```
1	---
2	name: quality-analysis
3	description: Comprehensive quality analysis for BMad workflows and skills. Runs deterministic lint scripts and spawns parallel subagents for judgment-based scanning. Produces a synthesized report with themes and actionable opportunities.
4	menu-code: QA
5	---
6	
7	# Quality Analysis
8	
9	Communicate with user in `{communication_language}`. Write report content in `{document_output_language}`.
10	
11	You orchestrate quality analysis on a BMad workflow or skill. Deterministic checks run as scripts (fast, zero tokens). Judgment-based analysis runs as LLM subagents. A report creator synthesizes everything into a unified, theme-based report.
12	
13	## Your Role: Coordination, Not File Reading
14	
15	**DO NOT read the target skill's files yourself.** Scripts and subagents do all analysis.
16	
17	You orchestrate: run deterministic scripts and pre-pass extractors, spawn LLM scanner subagents in parallel, then hand off to the report creator for synthesis.
18	
19	## Headless Mode
20	
21	If `{headless_mode}=true`, skip all user interaction, use safe defaults, note any warnings, and output structured JSON as specified in the Present Findings section.
22	
23	## Pre-Scan Checks
24	
25	Check for uncommitted changes. In headless mode, note warnings and proceed. In interactive mode, inform the user and confirm before proceeding. In interactive mode, also confirm the workflow is currently functioning.
26	
27	## Analysis Principles
28	
29	**Effectiveness over efficiency.** The analysis may suggest leaner phrasing, but if the current phrasing captures the right guidance, it should be kept. Over-optimization can make skills lose their effectiveness. The report presents opportunities — the user applies judgment.
30	
31	## Scanners
32	
33	### Lint Scripts (Deterministic — Run First)
34	
35	These run instantly, cost zero tokens, and produce structured JSON:
36	
37	| #   | Script                           | Focus                                   | Output File                |
38	| --- | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | -------------------------- |
39	| S1  | `scripts/scan-path-standards.py` | Path conventions                        | `path-standards-temp.json` |
40	| S2  | `scripts/scan-scripts.py`        | Script portability, PEP 723, unit tests | `scripts-temp.json`        |
41	
42	### Pre-Pass Scripts (Feed LLM Scanners)
43	
44	Extract metrics so LLM scanners work from compact data instead of raw files:
45	
46	| #   | Script                                  | Feeds                        | Output File                       |
47	| --- | --------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
48	| P1  | `scripts/prepass-workflow-integrity.py` | workflow-integrity scanner   | `workflow-integrity-prepass.json` |
49	| P2  | `scripts/prepass-prompt-metrics.py`     | prompt-craft scanner         | `prompt-metrics-prepass.json`     |
50	| P3  | `scripts/prepass-execution-deps.py`     | execution-efficiency scanner | `execution-deps-prepass.json`     |
51	
52	### LLM Scanners (Judgment-Based — Run After Scripts)
53	
54	Each scanner writes a free-form analysis document (not JSON):
55	
56	| #   | Scanner                                     | Focus                                                                     | Pre-Pass? | Output File                             |
57	| --- | ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------- | --------------------------------------- |
58	| L1  | `quality-scan-workflow-integrity.md`        | Structural completeness, naming, type-appropriate requirements            | Yes       | `workflow-integrity-analysis.md`        |
59	| L2  | `quality-scan-prompt-craft.md`              | Token efficiency, outcome-driven balance, progressive disclosure, pruning | Yes       | `prompt-craft-analysis.md`              |
60	| L3  | `quality-scan-execution-efficiency.md`      | Parallelization, subagent delegation, context optimization                | Yes       | `execution-efficiency-analysis.md`      |
61	| L4  | `quality-scan-skill-cohesion.md`            | Stage flow, purpose alignment, complexity appropriateness                 | No        | `skill-cohesion-analysis.md`            |
62	| L5  | `quality-scan-enhancement-opportunities.md` | Edge cases, UX gaps, user journeys, headless potential                    | No        | `enhancement-opportunities-analysis.md` |
63	| L6  | `quality-scan-script-opportunities.md`      | Deterministic operations that should be scripts                           | No        | `script-opportunities-analysis.md`      |
64	
65	## Execution
66	
67	First create output directory: `{bmad_builder_reports}/{skill-name}/quality-analysis/{date-time-stamp}/`
68	
69	### Step 1: Run All Scripts (Parallel)
70	
71	Run all lint scripts and pre-pass scripts in parallel:
72	
73	```bash
74	python3 scripts/scan-path-standards.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/path-standards-temp.json
75	python3 scripts/scan-scripts.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/scripts-temp.json
76	uv run scripts/prepass-workflow-integrity.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/workflow-integrity-prepass.json
77	python3 scripts/prepass-prompt-metrics.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/prompt-metrics-prepass.json
78	uv run scripts/prepass-execution-deps.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/execution-deps-prepass.json
79	```
80	
81	### Step 2: Spawn LLM Scanners (Parallel)
82	
83	After scripts complete, spawn all applicable LLM scanners as parallel subagents.
84	
85	**For scanners WITH pre-pass (L1, L2, L3):** provide the pre-pass JSON file path so the scanner reads compact metrics first, then reads raw files only as needed for judgment calls.
86	
87	**For scanners WITHOUT pre-pass (L4, L5, L6):** provide just the skill path and output directory.
88	
89	Each subagent receives:
90	
91	- Scanner file to load
92	- Skill path: `{skill-path}`
93	- Output directory: `{report-dir}`
94	- Pre-pass file path (if applicable)
95	
96	The subagent loads the scanner file, analyzes the skill, writes its analysis to the output directory, and returns the filename.
97	
98	### Step 3: Synthesize Report
99	
100	After all scanners complete, spawn a subagent with `./report-quality-scan-creator.md`.
101	
102	Provide:
103	
104	- `{skill-path}` — The skill being analyzed
105	- `{quality-report-dir}` — Directory containing all scanner output
106	
107	The report creator reads everything, synthesizes themes, and writes:
108	
109	1. `quality-report.md` — Narrative markdown report
110	2. `report-data.json` — Structured data for HTML
111	
112	### Step 4: Generate HTML Report
113	
114	After the report creator finishes, generate the interactive HTML:
115	
116	```bash
117	python3 scripts/generate-html-report.py {report-dir} --open
118	```
119	
120	This reads `report-data.json` and produces `quality-report.html` — a self-contained interactive report with opportunity themes, "Fix This Theme" prompt generation, and expandable detailed analysis.
121	
122	## Present to User
123	
124	**IF `{headless_mode}=true`:**
125	
126	Read `report-data.json` and output:
127	
128	```json
129	{
130	  "headless_mode": true,
131	  "scan_completed": true,
132	  "report_file": "{path}/quality-report.md",
133	  "html_report": "{path}/quality-report.html",
134	  "data_file": "{path}/report-data.json",
135	  "warnings": [],
136	  "grade": "Excellent|Good|Fair|Poor",
137	  "opportunities": 0,
138	  "broken": 0
139	}
140	```
141	
142	**IF interactive:**
143	
144	Read `report-data.json` and present:
145	
146	1. Grade and narrative — the 2-3 sentence synthesis
147	2. Broken items (if any) — critical/high issues prominently
148	3. Top opportunities — theme names with finding counts and impact
149	4. Reports — "Full report: quality-report.md" and "Interactive HTML opened in browser"
150	5. Offer: apply fixes directly, use HTML to select specific items, or discuss findings
151	
```

# `skf-brief-skill` Quality Analysis — Grade: **Good**

No critical/high defects. **25 findings** synthesized into **5 themes**, none broken.

## Top Opportunities

**1. Step-05 is a determinism leak** *(High, 4 findings)*
YAML emission, `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope, forge-tier `qmd_collections` mutation, and the brief write are all hand-templated in prose. Lifting them into scripts produces a JSON-schema-locked brief contract reusable by create-skill / update-skill / audit-skill. Biggest single win: ~500–800 tokens per invocation just on the YAML template.

**2. Failure-detection happens late** *(High, 5 findings)*
Repo-URL not pre-validated, semver not regex-checked at collection time, headless arg validation buried in prose, name-collision recovery undefined, `gh auth` runs reactively instead of upfront. Cuts ~5 minutes from worst-case recovery.

**3. The skill has opinions but doesn't share them** *(Medium, 4 findings)*
Five equal scope-type options instead of a recommended default; hidden defaults at confirmation; unexplained forge-tier choice; template-stamped descriptions slip through. Biggest first-timer UX win.

**4. Independent operations run sequentially** *(Medium, 4 findings)*
HEAD-checks on `doc_urls`, `gh api` calls in step-02, eager reference/asset loading, parent reading file content only to pipe into a script. ~25–30 KB context + 2–4s wall-time per run.

**5. Cosmetic polish** *(Low, 8 findings)*
Terminal-step keyword for step-06, five `please` → imperative conversions, dangling `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` reference, quote-punctuation in SKILL.md trigger phrase, table parenthetical, conditional restructures.

## Reports

- **Markdown:** `skills/reports/skf-brief-skill/quality-analysis/20260502-183504/quality-report.md`
- **Interactive HTML (opened in browser):** `skills/reports/skf-brief-skill/quality-analysis/20260502-183504/quality-report.html`
- **JSON:** `report-data.json`

1	# BMad Method · Quality Analysis: skf-brief-skill
2	
3	**Analyzed:** 2026-05-02T18:35:04Z | **Path:** /home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill
4	**Interactive report:** quality-report.html
5	
6	## Assessment
7	
8	**Good** — skf-brief-skill is a confidence-inspiring, highly cohered scoping workflow with first-class headless contracts, exemplary cross-step references, and a recently-completed migration of export extraction into a shared script. The primary opportunity is the next round of that same migration: step-05 still hand-templates YAML, the `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope, the registry mutation, and the file write in prose — all deterministic operations whose continued residency in the LLM costs ~1.2-2.8k tokens per run and creates schema-drift seams across the SKF pipeline.
9	
10	## What's Broken
11	
12	_No critical or high-severity defects. The only structural warning (terminal step missing progression keywords in step-06) is intentional behavior the heuristic flagged — see Opportunities §5._
13	
14	## Opportunities
15	
16	### 1. Step-05 is a determinism leak — YAML, envelope, registry, and write are all hand-templated (high — 4 observations)
17	
18	Step-05 is the heaviest single step (234 lines) and four of its sections do work that is purely deterministic: §3 hand-renders the `skill-brief.yaml` template with conditional optional fields and version-precedence rules; §4 writes non-atomically; §4b assembles `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` in prose at every success and HALT branch; §5 mutates `forge-tier.yaml`'s `qmd_collections` array directly. The LLM tax is real (~1k-1.5k tokens per invocation just from this step), but the architectural cost is bigger: each prose-rendered surface is a drift seam, and brief.yaml is the input contract for create-skill — partial writes cascade into a silently-broken pipeline.
19	
20	**Fix:** Land a `skf-write-skill-brief.py` (absorbs YAML emission, version-precedence, atomic-write, schema validation) and either generalize `skf-emit-result-envelope.py` with a `--workflow brief` mode or create a parallel `skf-emit-brief-result-envelope.py`. Add a `register-qmd-collection` subcommand to the existing `skf-forge-tier-rw.py`. The JSON Schema for the brief becomes a free CI lint and a consumer-side validator for create-skill / update-skill / audit-skill.
21	
22	**Observations:**
23	
24	- YAML emission of skill-brief.yaml hand-templated in prose (~500-800 tokens) — steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md:60-125 — script-opportunities
25	- Headless `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope assembled by LLM (~300-450 tokens; mirrors solved skf-setup pattern) — steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md:135-146 — script-opportunities
26	- `forge-tier.yaml` `qmd_collections` mutation in prose (Deep tier only, ~150-250 tokens) — steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md:177-190 — script-opportunities
27	- Non-atomic file write to skill-brief.yaml — correctness risk for the create-skill input contract — steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md:127-133 — script-opportunities
28	
29	### 2. Failure-detection happens late — invest validation up-front (high — 5 observations)
30	
31	Across the workflow, several inputs (repo URL existence, semver version format, headless arg shape, scope-collision recovery) are validated deep in the pipeline rather than at the point of capture. A confused user with a typo'd GitHub URL invests 5+ minutes of conversation before step-02 §1 catches it; a user typing `v2` or `2024.04.01` for `target_version` doesn't learn it isn't semver until step-05's invariant check; a headless caller supplying malformed `scope_type` reasons through enum validation in prose at the GATE; the existing-brief collision check tells the user "pick another name" with zero help picking. Pulling validation forward is the cheapest UX win available — and a `skf-validate-brief-inputs.py` pre-pass also collapses ~150-250 tokens of headless-GATE prose at step-01.
32	
33	**Fix:** Add a `skf-validate-brief-inputs.py` pre-pass that normalizes and validates the headless arg set against a JSON Schema, and use the same pattern interactively: HEAD-check repo URLs at step-01 §3 the same way doc URLs already are, regex-check semver at step-01 §3b, and on name collision auto-suggest alternates rather than just rejecting. The `gh auth status` call should also move from reactive (step-02 §4 after a failure) to preemptive (step-01 §3 the moment a GitHub URL is detected).
34	
35	**Observations:**
36	
37	- Repo URLs not pre-validated; failure surfaces 5+ minutes later in step-02 — steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:74 — enhancement-opportunities
38	- `target_version` not regex-validated as semver at collection time — steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:90 — enhancement-opportunities
39	- Headless input validation reasoned through in prose at GATE (~150-250 tokens, ~16 input fields) — steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:213 — script-opportunities / prompt-craft
40	- Brief-name collision tells user "pick another" with no recovery help — steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:156 — enhancement-opportunities
41	- `gh auth status` runs reactively after failure rather than preemptively — steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md:43-46 — execution-efficiency
42	
43	### 3. The skill has opinions but doesn't share them with the user (medium — 4 observations)
44	
45	SKILL.md openly states the skill "steers toward the smaller, sharper version when scope is unclear" and that "covers too much is a worse failure mode than covers too little" — but that opinion is invisible at decision time. step-03 §2c presents `[F] [M] [P] [C] [R]` as five equal options with no recommendation; step-04 §3 hides `scripts_intent`/`assets_intent` defaults the user is implicitly accepting; the welcome banner names a forge tier the first-timer cannot interpret; step-01 §7b's description-synthesis template will produce uniform bot-prose across the registry. The single highest-impact UX change is to let the LLM use what it already knows from step-02 to recommend an answer at every menu — keeping the menu, but pre-selecting the workflow's best guess.
46	
47	**Fix:** At step-03 §2c, have the LLM recommend a scope-type based on step-02's analysis output and the user's intent, with the other options preserved as overrides. At step-04 §3, always display Scripts/Assets/forge-tier values with one-line glosses (including for `detect`). Replace step-01 §7b's structured template with 2-3 example descriptions of varied voice and instruct the LLM to "write like a human library maintainer."
48	
49	**Observations:**
50	
51	- step-03 §2c presents five scope-type options as equal-weight; no recommended default — steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md:78 — enhancement-opportunities
52	- step-04 §3 hides `scripts_intent`/`assets_intent` when set to default `detect` — user agrees to behavior they were never shown — steps-c/step-04-confirm-brief.md (§3) — skill-cohesion
53	- Forge tier surfaced to user without explanation of capability differences — steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:213 + step-04 §3 — enhancement-opportunities
54	- step-01 §7b description template risks bot-prose homogeneity across registry — steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md (§7b) — enhancement-opportunities
55	
56	### 4. Independent operations run sequentially — explicit batching guidance is missing (medium — 4 observations)
57	
58	Several intra-step operations are independent but read top-to-bottom as sequential loops. doc-URL HEAD-checks (3-10 URLs typically) run one at a time in step-01 §3 and step-03 §2b; `gh api` calls in step-02 §1 + §4 issue the repo-existence check, the tree fetch, and per-file `contents` calls one round trip at a time; reference/asset files (`version-resolution.md`, `skill-brief-schema.md`, `scope-templates.md`) load eagerly at step entry even when their content is only conditionally needed. The fix is partly prose ("issue these in one message with parallel Bash calls") and partly architectural — extending `skf-extract-public-api.py` to fetch its own files would remove the parent's read of file content entirely.
59	
60	**Fix:** Add explicit "issue all N HEAD-checks in a single message with parallel Bash calls" guidance at step-01 §3 / step-03 §2b. Same for step-02 §4's manifest + entry-file fetches. Codify "load reference X only when entering branch Y" in SKILL.md Workflow Rules — the script-path / fallback split in step-02 §4 already does this correctly. Consider extending `skf-extract-public-api.py` with a `--repo + --ref` mode so it does its own `gh api` fetches and the parent never sees raw file content.
61	
62	**Observations:**
63	
64	- Sequential `curl -sI` HEAD checks across `doc_urls` — should batch — steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:77-79 + steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md:70 — execution-efficiency
65	- `gh api` calls in step-02 §1 + §4 issued sequentially — should batch — steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md:27-29 + 121-123 — execution-efficiency
66	- Reference/asset files eagerly loaded at step entry even when not needed (~25-30 KB per run) — steps-c/step-02:193, step-c/step-05:26, steps-c/step-03:78 — execution-efficiency
67	- Parent reads file content into context only to hand it to a script via stdin (drift seam) — steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md:121-143 — execution-efficiency
68	
69	### 5. Cosmetic / style polish — terminal-step keyword, please-imperatives, dangling reference (low — 8 observations)
70	
71	A handful of low-risk surface fixes: step-06's terminal completion is documented in prose but uses vocabulary the integrity-prepass scanner doesn't recognize; five user-facing prompts use `Please …` where direct imperative is the SKF convention; a `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` reference points to a file that doesn't exist (likely a refactor leftover); the SKILL.md description has inconsistent quote punctuation; the stage table has a long parenthetical that breaks visual rhythm; step-01 §3 weaves three nested conditionals together in a way an agent could mis-order; step-01 §8 is a 280-word paragraph swallowing 12+ headless arguments; step-02 §4 mixes three conditional paths in one section.
72	
73	**Fix:** Add a one-line "Completion criteria" sentence to step-06 using the keyword vocabulary; convert the five `Please …` prompts to direct imperatives; resolve or inline the `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` reference; close the trailing-period inconsistency in SKILL.md:3; move the parenthetical out of the stage table; restructure step-01 §3 into 3a/3b/3c sub-sections matching the §3b precedent; convert the §8 GATE paragraph into a "required (HALT if missing) / optional" table; split step-02 §4 into 4a/4b/4c.
74	
75	**Observations:**
76	
77	- step-06 terminal step has no progression-condition keywords (intentional but flagged) — steps-c/step-06-health-check.md (line 28) — workflow-integrity
78	- Five user-facing `Please …` prompts should be direct imperatives — steps-c/step-01:39,120; step-02:51; step-04:56; step-05:132 — workflow-integrity
79	- Dangling reference to missing `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` — steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md:180 — prompt-craft
80	- Trailing period inside quoted trigger phrase inconsistent — SKILL.md:3 — workflow-integrity
81	- Long parenthetical in stage table breaks visual rhythm — SKILL.md:39 — prompt-craft
82	- step-01 §3's three nested conditionals can be mis-ordered by an executing agent — steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:74-122 — prompt-craft
83	- step-01 §8 GATE is a single 280-word paragraph swallowing 12+ args — steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md:213 — prompt-craft
84	- step-02 §4 mixes three conditional paths in one section — steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md:116-167 — prompt-craft
85	
86	## Strengths
87	
88	- **Overview earns its tokens.** SKILL.md's three-paragraph Overview articulates what the skill produces, what good scope looks like, why brief-skill is split from create-skill, and the failure-mode asymmetry — all load-bearing for downstream agent judgment.
89	- **Headless contract is first-class, not an afterthought.** Exit codes table, `halt_reason` enum, per-step `GATE [default: ...]` blocks, and a single-line stdout/stderr `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope mean a CI pipeline can branch on the outcome without grepping prose. The HALT-on-stderr / success-on-stdout split is exactly right for shell pipelines.
90	- **Cross-step references done right.** `references/version-resolution.md` is loaded by step-01 §3b, step-02 §4b, and step-05 §3 with the consumer/producer responsibilities documented inside the reference itself. Worth holding up as a model for sibling skills.
91	- **Explicit description synthesis (step-01 §7b).** The workflow refuses to let `description` be implicit — synthesizing while intent is fresh, then re-confirming in step-04, prevents the "filler description" anti-pattern. The headless fallback log line (`"warn: description synthesized without intent — narrow registry text."`) is exactly the kind of operator-visible signal that prevents silent quality degradation.
92	- **Script delegation is justified, not just declared.** step-02 §4 explains *why* `skf-extract-public-api.py` exists ("Hand-rolling these in prose creates drift seams the LLM cannot fully close") so future maintainers respect the boundary instead of re-introducing prose duplicates.
93	- **Re-entry from step-04 [R] preserves prior selections.** step-03 §0 Rules explicitly lists which fields are preserved on re-entry — a subtle UX detail most workflows get wrong by silently resetting state.
94	- **Terminal-step discipline.** step-05 §7 explicitly warns "the user-facing success summary is NOT the terminal step" and step-06's CRITICAL note locks the loop closed. Prevents the "agent stops at the success summary" regression.
95	- **Graceful degradation everywhere.** forge-tier corrupted → fallback to Quick; ccc remote → skip with note; uv missing → prose-fallback; QMD embed fails → mark `status: pending`; tree truncated → offer local-clone recovery. This skill is genuinely good at this pattern.
96	- **Three distinct exit codes for the three GitHub-failure classes** (auth / 404 / missing binary) — automators can branch correctly on each.
97	- **Lint scans clean.** Path-standards: 0 findings. Scripts: no `scripts/` dir to scan (deliberate — this skill is a consumer of shared scripts, not an author of its own).
98	
99	## Detailed Analysis
100	
101	### Structure & Integrity
102	
103	Structurally the skill is in very good shape: well-formed complex workflow, all six referenced stage files present, sequential numbering, config headers on every stage, an unusually rich Invocation/Exit-Codes/Result contract that headless automators can rely on. The only structural concern is the absence of explicit progression keywords in the terminal step — intentional but the prepass heuristic doesn't recognize the prose form. Frontmatter is clean (name matches folder, two-part description with quoted trigger phrases), workflow-type claim matches structure, activation sequence is correctly ordered. No template artifacts, no orphaned conditionals.
104	
105	### Craft & Writing Quality
106	
107	Strong. The Overview is appropriately rich (3 paragraphs doing 4 distinct jobs); progressive disclosure is excellent (SKILL.md ~1.6k tokens, step bodies in `steps-c/`, templates in `assets/`, cross-cutting rules in `references/`). 7/7 prompts have config headers, progression conditions, and self-containment — strong context-compaction survival. Findings here are improvements, not rework: a dangling reference to a missing `knowledge/tool-resolution.md`, the heavy YAML template inline in step-05 §3 (which duplicates `assets/skill-brief-schema.md`), three nested conditionals in step-01 §3 that can be mis-ordered, and the §8 GATE paragraph that buries 12+ args in 280 words.
108	
109	### Cohesion & Design
110	
111	Highly cohered. Stage flow is a textbook discovery → analysis → decision → confirmation → write → verify chain; earlier stages produce exactly what later stages consume; back-edge from step-04 [R] → step-03 is documented and state-preserving. Purpose alignment is strong (every step earns its place against the SKILL.md mission), with one subtle gap: the QMD collection registration in step-05 §5 and the chain to step-06 health-check are not hinted at in the Overview. Complexity is on the upper end of appropriate but earned. The dependency graph has zero cycles, the headless contract integrity is exact, and external skill integrations (`bmad-advanced-elicitation`, `bmad-party-mode`, `shared/health-check.md`, `skf-extract-public-api.py`, `qmd`) are all optional or well-bounded.
112	
113	Cohesion findings (not in themes above): the schema's `source_ref` lifecycle is invisible to the brief author (target_version implicitly drives downstream tag resolution); no programmatic schema-validation pass between assembly and write; `tier_source` diagnostic is write-only.
114	
115	### Execution Efficiency
116	
117	By design, this is an interactive scoping conversation — not a parallelizable batch workflow. Pre-pass found zero dependency-graph issues, no subagent-from-subagent patterns, no diamond hazards. The highest-leverage efficiency move (delegating manifest parsing to a script) is already in place. The remaining wins are intra-step batching of independent operations (HEAD-checks, `gh api` calls), lazy reference loading on conditional branches, lighter-weight re-renders on inline edits, and a preemptive `gh auth status` check.
118	
119	### User Experience
120	
121	Headless-ready, bordering on production-grade headless from day one — every gate has a `GATE [default: ...]` clause, inputs map cleanly from CLI args to brief fields, the result envelope is machine-parseable, exit codes are stable. Auto-resolvable interaction points are exhaustively enumerated. The headless gaps are real but narrow: no `description` direct-override (only via `intent` synthesis), no `--dry-run` mode, no streaming progress signal for long runs.
122	
123	The interactive experience is comprehensive but optimizes for the expert and the automator over the first-timer. Five user archetypes were traced: first-timer (hits jargon walls and unrecommended menus), expert (no "yolo" middle ground between full ceremony and full headless), confused user (no clean exit path, late URL validation), edge-case user (no multi-workspace selection, semver-only versions), hostile environment (HEAD-check has no timeout, `qmd embed` could hang). Six facilitative-pattern gaps surface: most valuable additions are Parallel Review Lenses before step-04 (directly attacks the "scope too broad" failure mode), Capture-Don't-Interrupt in step-03, and a Soft Gate at end of step-01 §4.
124	
125	### Script Opportunities
126	
127	The skill has no `scripts/` of its own — by design, it's a consumer of shared SKF scripts, and the recent commit `12eb1dc4 refactor(skf-brief-skill): wire skf-extract-public-api.py into step-02` shows a deliberate ongoing migration of deterministic work out of prose. Ten unmigrated determinism leaks remain, totaling ~1,200-1,800 tokens per typical interactive run (~1,950-2,800 tokens for headless + Deep tier + monorepo). The four step-05 items in Theme 1 are the highest-leverage; the input-validation pre-pass in Theme 2 is the architecturally cleanest standalone addition. Findings 9 (monorepo detection) and 10 (language detection) should be added as modes to the existing `skf-extract-public-api.py` rather than separate scripts — collapsing three LLM-mediated handoffs into one deterministic pipeline.
128	
129	**Token savings:** ~1,200-1,800 (typical) to ~1,950-2,800 (headless + Deep + monorepo) per invocation.
130	
131	## Recommendations
132	
133	1. **Land `skf-write-skill-brief.py` (absorbs YAML emission, version-precedence, atomic write, schema validation).** Resolves 4 observations in Theme 1; produces a JSON Schema artifact that doubles as a consumer-side validator for the entire SKF pipeline. Effort: medium.
134	2. **Pull validation forward across the workflow (URL pre-check, semver regex, `skf-validate-brief-inputs.py` pre-pass, preemptive `gh auth status`, name-collision auto-suggest).** Resolves 5 observations in Theme 2; biggest UX win for the recovery-from-mistake path. Effort: medium.
135	3. **Add explicit batching guidance and lazy reference loading (parallel HEAD-checks, batched `gh api` calls, conditional `version-resolution.md` load, codified Workflow Rule).** Resolves 4 observations in Theme 4; ~25-30 KB context savings + 2-4 seconds wall-time. Effort: low.
136	4. **Have the LLM share the skill's opinions at decision time (recommended scope-type, always-shown defaults, forge-tier glosses, voice-varied description examples).** Resolves 4 observations in Theme 3; biggest first-timer UX win. Effort: low.
137	5. **Generalize `skf-emit-result-envelope.py` with a `--workflow brief` mode (or parallel `skf-emit-brief-result-envelope.py`).** Resolves the highest-architectural-consistency item in Theme 1; the proven skf-setup pattern applies one-to-one. Effort: low.
138	6. **Add a `register-qmd-collection` subcommand to existing `skf-forge-tier-rw.py`.** Pure extension of an existing primitive; centralizes a registry mutation that 5+ SKF skills will need. Effort: low.
139	7. **Cosmetic/style polish pass (terminal-step keyword, please→imperative, dangling reference, table parenthetical, §3 sub-sectioning, §8 table form, §4 a/b/c split).** Resolves 8 observations in Theme 5; mostly mechanical. Effort: low.
140	8. **Optional UX additions: `--quick` interactive mode, `--dry-run` headless mode, `description` direct-override arg, `[Q] Quit` partial-state save.** Bridges the two-mode (interactive / headless) split into a continuum. Effort: medium.
141

1	---
2	name: skf-brief-skill
3	description: Design a skill scope through guided discovery. Use when the user requests to "create a skill brief" or "brief a skill."
4	---
5	
6	# Brief Skill
7	
8	## Overview
9	
10	Helps the user define what to skill — target repo, scope, language, inclusion/exclusion patterns — and produces a `skill-brief.yaml` that drives create-skill. This is the first step in the skill creation pipeline; the brief is the input contract for create-skill, which performs the actual compilation.
11	
12	A good skill brief sets a tight, cohesive boundary: one capability with 3-8 primary functions, an unambiguous public API surface, and a description short enough to fit in a registry row. Briefs that try to cover several unrelated concerns (e.g. authentication *and* data visualization) compile into skills that no agent can route to confidently — a brief covering too much is a worse failure mode than a brief covering too little, and this workflow steers toward the smaller, sharper version when scope is unclear.
13	
14	Brief-skill is split from create-skill so the scoping conversation runs *once*, on cheap signals (manifests, top-level exports, intent), without paying for AST extraction. Compilation is expensive; scoping decisions are cheap to revise. Keeping them in separate workflows lets a user iterate on the brief, share it for review, and re-run create-skill against the same brief whenever the upstream version moves.
15	
16	## Role
17	
18	You are a skill scoping architect collaborating with a developer who wants to create an agent skill. You bring expertise in source code analysis, API surface identification, and skill boundary design, while the user brings their domain knowledge and specific use case. Work together as equals.
19	
20	## Workflow Rules
21	
22	These rules apply to every step in this workflow:
23	
24	- Read each step file completely before taking any action
25	- Follow the mandatory sequence in each step exactly — do not skip, reorder, or optimize
26	- Only load one step file at a time — never preload future steps
27	- Always communicate in `{communication_language}`
28	- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with their default action and log each auto-decision
29	
30	## Stages
31	
32	| # | Step | File | Auto-proceed |
33	|---|------|------|--------------|
34	| 1 | Gather Intent | steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md | No (interactive) |
35	| 2 | Analyze Target | steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md | Yes |
36	| 3 | Scope Definition | steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md | No (interactive) |
37	| 4 | Confirm Brief | steps-c/step-04-confirm-brief.md | No (confirm) |
38	| 5 | Write Brief | steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md | Yes |
39	| 6 | Workflow Health Check (terminal — chains to `shared/health-check.md`) | steps-c/step-06-health-check.md | Yes |
40	
41	## Invocation Contract
42	
43	| Aspect | Detail |
44	|--------|--------|
45	| **Inputs** | `target_repo` [required], `skill_name` [required], `scope_hint` [optional], `language_hint` [optional], `target_version` [optional], `source_authority` [optional: official/community/internal, default community], `source_type` [optional: source/docs-only, default source], `doc_urls` [optional: list of `url[,label]` for source_type=docs-only or supplemental], `scope_type` [optional: full-library/specific-modules/public-api/component-library/reference-app/docs-only], `include` [optional: comma-separated globs], `exclude` [optional: comma-separated globs], `scripts_intent` [optional: detect/none/free-text, default detect], `assets_intent` [optional: detect/none/free-text, default detect], `intent` [optional: free-text used to derive description], `force` [optional: overwrite existing brief without prompting] |
46	| **Gates** | step-01: Input Gate [use args] | step-03: Confirm Gate [C] | step-04: Confirm Gate [C] |
47	| **Outputs** | `skill-brief.yaml` at `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml`; final `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` line on stdout when `{headless_mode}` is true |
48	| **Headless** | All gates auto-resolve with default action when `{headless_mode}` is true; pre-supplied inputs consumed at the gates that would otherwise prompt; existing briefs are preserved unless `--force` was supplied (HALT with `overwrite-cancelled` otherwise) |
49	| **Exit codes** | See "Exit Codes" below |
50	
51	## Exit Codes
52	
53	Every HARD HALT in this workflow exits with a stable code so headless automators can branch on the failure class without grepping message text:
54	
55	| Code | Meaning              | Raised by                                                                                  |
56	| ---- | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
57	| 0    | success              | step-06 (terminal)                                                                         |
58	| 2    | input-missing        | step-01 GATE — required headless arg absent (`target_repo`, `skill_name`)                  |
59	| 3    | resolution-failure   | step-01 §1 (`forge-tier.yaml` missing); step-02 §1 (target inaccessible / `gh auth` fails) |
60	| 4    | write-failure        | step-05 §4 (write to `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml` failed)           |
61	| 5    | overwrite-cancelled  | step-05 §2 (existing brief, `force` not supplied)                                          |
62	
63	## Result Contract (Headless)
64	
65	When `{headless_mode}` is true, step-05 emits a single-line JSON envelope on **stdout** before chaining to step-06, and every HARD HALT emits the same envelope shape on **stderr** with `status: "error"`:
66	
67	```
68	SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON: {"status":"success|error","brief_path":"…|null","skill_name":"…","version":"…|null","language":"…|null","scope_type":"…|null","exit_code":0,"halt_reason":null}
69	```
70	
71	`status` is `"success"` on the terminal happy path, `"error"` on any HALT. `halt_reason` is one of: `null` (success), `"input-missing"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`. `exit_code` matches the table above.
72	
73	## On Activation
74	
75	1. Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/config.yaml` and resolve:
76	   - `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`, `communication_language`, `forge_data_folder`, `sidecar_path`
77	
78	2. **Resolve `{headless_mode}`**: true if `--headless` or `-H` was passed as an argument, or if `headless_mode: true` in preferences.yaml. Default: false.
79	
80	3. Load, read the full file, and then execute `./steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md` to begin the workflow.
81

| Order | PR | Branch | Commits | Effort |

| 2 | **Theme 3: Surface skill opinions** | `feat/skf-brief-skill-surface-defaults` | 4 | Low — content edits to step-01/03/04 |
| 3 | **Theme 4: Parallel ops + lazy loads** | `perf/skf-brief-skill-parallel-batching` | 3–4 | Low — prose guidance + 1 SKILL.md rule |
| 4 | **Theme 2: Pull validation forward** | `feat/skf-brief-skill-fail-fast-validation` | 5 | Med — 1 new script (`skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`) + tests + URL/semver checks |
| 5 | **Theme 1: Step-05 → scripts** | `refactor/skf-brief-skill-step-05-scripts` | 4 | Med-High — new `skf-write-skill-brief.py` + `--workflow brief` mode on emit-result-envelope + `register-qmd-collection` subcommand on forge-tier-rw + JSON schema + tests |

### Per-PR rules
- Each PR runs `npm run quality` before push.
- Theme 1 & 2 add **tests** for new scripts (matching the `test-skf-extract-public-api.py` pattern in `test/`).
- Code-review pass with `feature-dev:code-reviewer` after Theme 1 & 2 (riskiest).
- After all 5 merged: end-to-end `feature-dev:code-reviewer` over the whole sweep for cross-theme regressions/impacts on consumer skills (create-skill, update-skill, audit-skill).
- No `Fixes #NNN` — these come from a quality report, not a GitHub issue.

### Why this order
Cosmetic & content-only first (Themes 5, 3, 4) lands quick wins, validates my CI loop, and keeps the high-risk PRs (2, 1) reviewable in isolation. Theme 1 last because it changes the brief.yaml emission contract — if anything breaks downstream it'll be that one.

### Note on dangling reference (Theme 5)
`src/knowledge/tool-resolution.md` actually exists. The reference in step-02:180 is `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` (relative), which doesn't resolve from `steps-c/`. The fix is path correction, not file creation.

### What I won't auto-do
- **Recommendation #8** in the report (`--quick`, `--dry-run`, `description` direct-override, `[Q] Quit`) — these are scope expansions, not findings. I'll skip unless you say otherwise.
- Writing code-review/agent-spawning into commits — those run as my workflow, not as artifacts.

Starting Theme 5 now unless you stop me.

6	
7	# Step 1: Gather Intent
8	
9	## STEP GOAL:
10	
11	To initialize the brief-skill workflow by discovering the forge tier configuration, then gathering the user's target repository, intent, and any upfront scope hints for skill creation.
12	
13	## Rules
14	
15	- Focus only on gathering intent — do not analyze the repo yet (Step 02)
16	- Do not examine source code or list exports in this step
17	- Open-ended discovery facilitation — collect target repo, user intent, scope hints, skill name
18	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`
19	
20	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
21	
22	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
23	
24	### 1. Discover Forge Tier
25	
26	Attempt to load `{forgeTierFile}`:
27	
28	**If found:**
29	- Read the tier level (quick, forge, forge+, or deep)
30	- Note available tools for scoping guidance later
31	
32	**Apply tier override:** Read `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml`. If `tier_override` is set and is a valid tier value (Quick, Forge, Forge+, or Deep), use it instead of the detected tier.
33	
34	**If found but the YAML cannot be parsed (corrupted or truncated):**
35	- Display: "**Cannot read forge-tier.yaml** at `{forgeTierFile}` — the file exists but failed to parse: `{parser error message}`. The setup workflow can rewrite it cleanly. Until then, the brief workflow falls back to **Quick** tier (no extra tools assumed)."
36	- Continue with `tier = "Quick"` and `tools = {}` — do not HALT. Record `tier_source: "fallback-corrupted-config"` for later diagnostics.
37	
38	**If not found:**
39	- "**Cannot proceed.** forge-tier.yaml not found at `{forgeTierFile}`. Please run the **setup** workflow first to configure your forge tier (Quick/Forge/Forge+/Deep)."
40	- HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "forge-tier-missing"`) — do not proceed.
41	
42	### 2. Welcome and Explain
43	
44	"**Welcome to Brief Skill — the skill scoping workflow.**
45	
46	I'll help you define exactly what to skill and produce a `skill-brief.yaml` that drives the create-skill compilation workflow.
47	
48	We'll work through this together:
49	1. **Now:** Understand what you want to skill and why
50	2. **Next:** Analyze the target repo structure
51	3. **Then:** Define scope boundaries
52	4. **Finally:** Confirm and write the brief
53	
54	{If tier override was applied:}
55	**Your forge tier:** {override tier} (overridden from {original tier}) — this determines what compilation capabilities are available.
56	{Else:}
57	**Your forge tier:** {detected tier} — this determines what compilation capabilities are available.
58	
59	Let's get started."
60	
61	### 3. Gather Target Repository
62	
63	"**What repository or documentation do you want to create a skill for?**
64	
65	Provide one of:
66	- A **GitHub URL** (e.g., `https://github.com/org/repo`)
67	- A **local path** (e.g., `/path/to/project`)
68	- **Documentation URLs** for a docs-only skill (e.g., `https://docs.stripe.com/api`) — use this when no source code is available (SaaS, closed-source)
69	
70	**Target:**"
71	
72	Wait for user response.
73	
74	**If user provides documentation URLs (not a repo):**
75	- Set `source_type: "docs-only"` in the brief data
76	- Collect one or more doc URLs with optional labels
77	- For each collected URL, perform a `HEAD` request (or `curl -sI`) to verify the URL resolves with a 2xx/3xx status:
78	  - On 2xx/3xx: silently accept.
79	  - On 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout: warn `"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}. Confirm the URL is correct, or proceed anyway."` Interactive: re-prompt for a corrected URL or `[K] Keep anyway`. Headless: keep the URL and log the warning — the brief still records it but the failure is now visible at brief-creation time instead of materializing hours later in skf-create-skill.
80	- Note: `source_authority` will be forced to `community` (T3 external documentation)
81	- Note: `source_repo` becomes optional (can be set to the main doc site URL for reference)
82	
83	**If user provides a GitHub URL or local path:**
84	- Set `source_type: "source"` (default)
85	- Optionally ask: "Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context? (These will be fetched as T3 external references.)"
86	- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`
87	
88	**Source authority (for all source-type skills):**
89	"**Are you the maintainer of this library, or creating a community skill?**"
90	- If maintainer: set `source_authority: "official"`
91	- If community user: set `source_authority: "community"` (default)
92	- If internal/proprietary: set `source_authority: "internal"`
93	
94	Default to `"community"` if user does not specify or skips. For `docs-only` skills, `source_authority` is always forced to `"community"`.
95	
96	Confirm the target.
97	
98	### 3b. Gather Target Version
99	
100	Load `{versionResolutionFile}` for the canonical precedence and invariant rules — this step only collects `target_version`; auto-detection runs in step 02 and resolution lands in step 05.
101	
102	**Headless:** if `target_version` was supplied as an argument, store it and skip the interactive prompt below. If `doc_urls` were also supplied, treat the version-vs-doc-URL confirmation prompt as auto-confirmed (Y).
103	
104	"**Are you targeting a specific version of this library?**
105	(Leave blank to auto-detect from source)"
106	
107	{If source_type is "docs-only":}
108	"Since this is a docs-only skill with no source code, specifying the version is recommended — otherwise it defaults to 1.0.0."
109	
110	Wait for user response.
111	
112	**If user provides a version:** Store as `target_version`. Set `version` to this value.
113	**If blank:** Proceed without `target_version` — version will be auto-detected in step 02.
114	
115	{If target_version was set AND doc_urls are being collected (either docs-only primary or supplemental):}
116	
117	"**You're targeting version {target_version}. Do these documentation URLs correspond to that version?** [Y/N]"
118	
119	- **If Y:** Proceed.
120	- **If N:** "Please provide the correct documentation URLs for version {target_version}." Re-collect doc_urls.
121	
122	### 4. Gather User Intent
123	
124	"**What's your intent for this skill?**
125	
126	Help me understand:
127	- **What** specifically do you want to skill from this repo?
128	- **Why** — what's the use case? How will an AI agent use this skill?
129	- **Any initial thoughts** on scope? (Full library? Specific modules? Public API only?)
130	
131	Take your time — the more context you share, the better the brief."
132	
133	Wait for user response. Ask follow-up questions if intent is unclear.
134	
135	### 5. Capture Scope Hints
136	
137	If the user mentioned scope preferences in their intent response, acknowledge them:
138	
139	"**I noted these scope hints from your response:**
140	- {list any scope hints mentioned}
141	
142	We'll refine these after analyzing the repo structure in the next step."
143	
144	If no scope hints were mentioned, that's fine — skip this acknowledgment.
145	
146	### 6. Derive Skill Name
147	
148	Based on the target repo and intent, propose a skill name:
149	
150	"**Suggested skill name:** `{derived-name}` (kebab-case)
151	
152	This will be used for the output directory and file naming. Want to use this name or suggest something different?"
153	
154	Wait for confirmation or alternative.
155	
156	**Collision check (interactive and headless):** before locking the name, check whether `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/skill-brief.yaml` already exists. If it does:
157	
158	- Interactive: "**Heads up — a brief for `{name}` already exists at `{path}`.** Pick a different name to keep the new brief separate, or confirm to continue (the existing brief's overwrite prompt fires in step 05)."
159	- Headless: log `"warn: skill name '{name}' collides with existing brief at {path}"` and proceed; the existing-brief overwrite policy in step-05 §2b is the canonical gate (HALT with `overwrite-cancelled` unless `force` was supplied).
160	
161	### 7. Summarize Gathered Intent
162	
163	"**Here's what I've captured:**
164	
165	- **Target:** {repo URL or path}
166	- **Intent:** {user's intent summary}
167	- **Scope hints:** {any hints, or "None — we'll define scope after analysis"}
168	- **Skill name:** {confirmed name}
169	- **Source type:** {source or docs-only}
170	- **Source authority:** {official/community/internal}
171	{If target_version set:}
172	- **Target version:** {target_version} (user-specified)
173	{If doc_urls collected:}
174	- **Doc URLs:** {count} supplemental documentation URLs
175	- **Forge tier:** {tier}
176	
177	Ready to analyze the target repository?"
178	
179	### 7b. Synthesize Skill Description
180	
181	The schema's `description` field is 1-3 sentences and surfaces in skill registries — it must exist by the time step-04 presents the brief. Synthesize it explicitly here, while the user's intent is fresh, instead of letting it fall out implicitly later.
182	
183	Compose a candidate 1-3 sentence description from the gathered material. Pattern (adjust naturally — do not template-stamp):
184	
185	```
186	{What the skill enables an agent to do, in active voice}, drawn from {target}{ at version {target_version}, if set}{ for {scope hint summary}, if any scope hints were captured}. Use when {one-sentence trigger condition derived from intent — what task or question routes to this skill}.
187	```
188	
189	Present:
190	
191	"**Proposed skill description:**
192	
193	> {synthesized description}
194	
195	This is what shows up when agents discover the skill. Edit it, replace it, or accept as-is."
196	
197	Wait for user confirmation or alternative. Store the accepted text as the brief's `description` field. The same field is re-presented in step-04 §4 for a final review pass — refinements there flow back to this value.
198	
199	**Headless:** if the `intent` argument was supplied, run the same synthesis against it and store the result. If `intent` was not supplied, derive from `target_repo` + `skill_name` (`"Use the {skill_name} skill to work with code or content from {target_repo}."`) and log `"warn: description synthesized without intent — narrow registry text."`
200	
201	### 8. Present MENU OPTIONS
202	
203	Display: "**Select:** [C] Continue to Target Analysis"
204	
205	#### Menu Handling Logic:
206	
207	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
208	- IF Any other: Help user, then [Redisplay Menu Options](#8-present-menu-options)
209	
210	#### EXECUTION RULES:
211	
212	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
213	- **GATE [default: use args]** — If `{headless_mode}`: consume pre-supplied arguments and auto-proceed. Required: `target_repo`, `skill_name`. Optional, applied to the matching brief field when present: `scope_hint`, `language_hint`, `target_version`, `source_authority` (default `community`), `source_type` (default `source`; if `docs-only`, `doc_urls` becomes required), `doc_urls` (list of `url` or `url,label`), `scope_type`, `include`, `exclude`, `scripts_intent` (default `detect`), `assets_intent` (default `detect`), `intent` (free-text used to derive `description`). If `target_repo` or `skill_name` is missing, HALT with exit code 2 (`halt_reason: "input-missing"`): "headless mode requires target_repo and skill_name arguments." If `source_type=docs-only` and no `doc_urls` supplied, same HALT with `halt_reason: "input-missing"`.
214	- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
215	
216	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
217	
218	ONLY WHEN C is selected and target repository is confirmed will you load and read fully `./step-02-analyze-target.md` to execute target analysis.
219	
220

1	---
2	nextStepFile: './step-03-scope-definition.md'
3	versionResolutionFile: 'references/version-resolution.md'
4	extractPublicApiScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-extract-public-api.py'
5	---
6	
7	# Step 2: Analyze Target
8	
9	## STEP GOAL:
10	
11	To analyze the target repository by resolving its location, reading its structure, detecting the primary language, and listing top-level modules and exports — providing the user with a factual foundation for scoping decisions.
12	
13	## Rules
14	
15	- Focus only on analysis — do not define scope yet (Step 03)
16	- Do not make scoping decisions or recommendations
17	- Do not hallucinate or guess about repository contents
18	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`
19	
20	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
21	
22	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
23	
24	### 1. Resolve Target Location
25	
26	**For GitHub URLs:**
27	- Use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` to verify the repository exists
28	- Use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/HEAD?recursive=1` to get the file tree
29	
30	**Truncation detection:** After receiving the tree response, check the `truncated` field in the JSON output. If `truncated: true`:
31	- Display: "Note: GitHub API returned a truncated tree response ({count} items). Full analysis may require a local clone."
32	- Record in analysis summary: "Tree listing is partial — some files may not appear in the analysis."
33	- For very large repos (>1000 files in tree response): offer a recovery path instead of just warning. Interactive — present:
34	  ```
35	  Tree is truncated. How would you like to proceed?
36	    [L] Clone locally and re-analyze (slower but complete)
37	    [P] Proceed with the partial tree (faster, may miss exports under deeper paths)
38	  ```
39	  On `[L]`: shallow-clone (`git clone --depth 1 {url} {tmp_dir}`), restart this section against the local path, and remove `{tmp_dir}` after the analysis summary in §5. On `[P]` (or under headless): record `tree_truncated: true` in the analysis summary and continue without HALT.
40	
41	**On API failure (non-200 from `gh api`):**
42	
43	Distinguish the failure class before reporting:
44	- Auto-run `gh auth status` and capture its output. If it reports an unauthenticated state or expired token: HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "gh-auth-failed"`) — "**Error:** GitHub CLI is not authenticated. `gh auth status` says: `{captured output}`. Run `gh auth login` and retry."
45	- If `gh auth status` reports authenticated but the call still failed (404/403): HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "target-inaccessible"`) — "**Error:** Cannot access repository at `{url}`. The CLI is authenticated but the API returned `{status}`. Check the URL and that the account has access to private repositories if applicable."
46	- If `gh auth status` itself fails to run (binary missing): HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "gh-auth-failed"`) — "**Error:** `gh` CLI not found on PATH. Install it from <https://cli.github.com> and re-run."
47	
48	**For local paths:**
49	- Verify the directory exists
50	- List the directory tree
51	- If path doesn't exist: **HALT** — "**Error:** Directory not found at {path}. Please verify the path is correct."
52	
53	Display: "**Resolving target...**"
54	
55	### 1b. Detect Monorepo / Workspace Layout
56	
57	Before listing the structure, check whether the root manifest declares a workspace layout:
58	
59	- **JavaScript/TypeScript:** root `package.json` has a `"workspaces"` array, OR a `pnpm-workspace.yaml` / `lerna.json` exists at the root.
60	- **Rust:** root `Cargo.toml` has `[workspace]` with a `members = [...]` list.
61	- **Python:** repo contains multiple `pyproject.toml` under `packages/*/` or `apps/*/`.
62	- **Generic:** top-level `apps/`, `packages/`, `code/`, or `libs/` directory each containing manifests.
63	
64	If a workspace layout is detected, list the discovered workspaces and ask:
65	
66	```
67	This looks like a monorepo with these workspaces:
68	  1. {workspace_name} ({path})
69	  2. {workspace_name} ({path})
70	  ...
71	Which one should the skill cover? Pick a number, type 'all' to scope at the repo root, or 'list' to keep listing more.
72	```
73	
74	Interactive: wait for the user choice. On a numbered choice, store `monorepo_workspace: {path}` and rebase §2-§4b against that path. On `'all'`, leave `monorepo_workspace` unset and proceed at the repo root with a note in the analysis summary that scope is unfiltered.
75	
76	Headless: if the input contract supplied an `include` glob that begins with one of the workspace paths, auto-select that workspace (log `"headless: auto-selected workspace {name} from include glob"`). Otherwise default to repo root and log `"warn: monorepo detected but no workspace pre-selected — analyzing at repo root"`.
77	
78	If no workspace layout is detected, skip this section silently.
79	
80	### 2. Read Repository Structure
81	
82	List the top-level directory structure:
83	
84	"**Repository Structure:**
85	```
86	{repo-name}/
87	├── {top-level files}
88	├── {top-level directories}/
89	│   └── ...
90	└── ...
91	```
92	**Total:** {file count} files, {directory count} directories"
93	
94	### 3. Detect Primary Language
95	
96	Examine file extensions and configuration files to detect the primary language:
97	
98	**Detection signals (check in order):**
99	1. `package.json` → JavaScript/TypeScript
100	2. `tsconfig.json` → TypeScript
101	3. `Cargo.toml` → Rust
102	4. `pyproject.toml` or `setup.py` or `setup.cfg` → Python
103	5. `go.mod` → Go
104	6. `pom.xml` or `build.gradle` → Java
105	7. `*.csproj` or `*.sln` → C#
106	8. `Gemfile` → Ruby
107	9. File extension frequency analysis as fallback
108	
109	"**Detected language:** {language}
110	**Confidence:** {high/medium/low}
111	**Detection source:** {what config file or pattern confirmed it}"
112	
113	If confidence is low or ambiguous: flag for user override in step 03.
114	
115	### 4. List Top-Level Modules and Exports
116	
117	Identify the public API surface. **Delegate the parsing to `{extractPublicApiScript}` whenever the detected language is supported** — the script is the single source of truth for manifest parsing, export discovery, and version detection across the whole SKF pipeline. Hand-rolling these in prose creates drift seams the LLM cannot fully close.
118	
119	**Script-supported languages** (use the script): `js`, `ts`, `javascript`, `typescript`, `python`, `rust`, `go`, `java`, `kotlin`.
120	
121	**Procedure when supported:**
122	
123	1. Read the relevant files into memory (no parsing yet — just collect content). For GitHub sources use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{file}` with base64 decode; for local sources read directly.
124	
125	   | Language | Manifest | Entry points (mode=quick) |
126	   |----------|----------|--------------------------|
127	   | js / ts / javascript / typescript | `package.json` (root, or primary workspace package per `references/version-resolution.md`) | `index.{ts,js}` and/or `src/index.{ts,js}` if present |
128	   | python | `pyproject.toml` (or `setup.py` / `setup.cfg` if no `pyproject.toml`) | top-level `__init__.py` of the package, plus `_version.py` if present |
129	   | rust | `Cargo.toml` (`[package]` — workspace root if `version = { workspace = true }`) | `src/lib.rs` |
130	   | go | `go.mod` | top-level `*.go` exporting the package surface |
131	   | java | `pom.xml` | (manifest alone is sufficient for the modules listing) |
132	   | kotlin | `build.gradle` / `build.gradle.kts` | (manifest alone) |
133	
134	2. Build a JSON payload matching the script contract:
135	
136	   ```json
137	   {
138	     "language": "<one of the supported values>",
139	     "manifest": {"path": "<relative path>", "content": "<file contents>"},
140	     "entries":  [{"path": "<relative path>", "content": "<file contents>"}, ...],
141	     "mode":     "quick"
142	   }
143	   ```
144	
145	3. Invoke the script and parse its JSON stdout:
146	
147	   ```bash
148	   echo '<payload-json>' | uv run {extractPublicApiScript} --language <lang> --mode quick
149	   ```
150	
151	   On a non-zero exit (codes 1 or 2 per the script's docstring), capture stderr, log it, and fall through to the prose-fallback path below — never HALT just because the script choked on an unusual manifest.
152	
153	4. Render the returned `package_name`, `exports` (each entry's `name`/`type`/`source_file`), `dependencies`, and any `warnings` to the user. The script also returns `version` — feed that into §4b instead of re-deriving.
154	
155	5. The script does not enumerate directories under `src/`. The LLM still lists those as "Top-Level Modules/Directories" so the user sees structural context (Maven and Gradle are the exception — for those, the script returns a `modules` array which IS the list).
156	
157	**Procedure when not supported** (Ruby / C# / Swift / etc.):
158	
159	Fall back to ad-hoc inspection — `Gemfile` / `*.csproj` / `*.sln` / `Package.swift` / file extension frequency. List top-level source directories as potential modules and note any obvious entry points. Flag the limitation in the analysis summary so the user knows scoping is on coarser signals.
160	
161	**Output format (both paths):**
162	
163	"**Top-Level Modules/Directories:**
164	{numbered list of modules with brief description of each}
165	
166	**Detected Exports/Entry Points:**
167	{numbered list of public-facing items found — from script output when available, ad-hoc inspection otherwise}"
168	
169	**Semantic Signals (Forge+ and Deep with ccc only):**
170	
171	**Remote source guard:** If the target source was resolved via GitHub API (remote URL, not a local file path), skip this CCC subsection — CCC requires a local source index and cannot operate on remote-only sources. Note: "CCC semantic discovery skipped — target is remote. CCC discovery will run automatically during create-skill after the source is cloned."
172	
173	If `tools.ccc` is true in forge-tier.yaml, supplement the module listing with a semantic discovery pass:
174	
175	**CCC Semantic Discovery:**
176	- **Claude Code:** Use `/ccc search "{repo_name} public API exports modules" {source_path}`
177	- **Cursor:** Use `ccc` MCP server `search` tool with query `"{repo_name} public API exports modules"` and path `{source_path}`
178	- **CLI fallback:** `ccc search "{repo_name} public API exports modules" --path {source_path} --limit 10`
179	
180	See `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` for full bridge-to-tool mapping.
181	
182	If results are returned, display:
183	
184	"**Semantic Signals (ccc):**
185	{numbered list of file:snippet pairs from CCC results — top 5 most relevant}"
186	
187	This supplements — never replaces — the explicit module list above. CCC may surface non-obvious entry points (dynamically constructed exports, re-export chains) that static directory analysis misses.
188	
189	If CCC is unavailable or returns no results: skip this subsection silently.
190	
191	### 4b. Detect Source Version
192	
193	Load `{versionResolutionFile}` for the canonical precedence and invariant rules.
194	
195	**When the language was script-supported (§4 took the script path):** the `version` field returned by `{extractPublicApiScript}` IS the detected version — do not re-derive it. The script already implements the language-specific lookups documented in `{versionResolutionFile}`.
196	
197	**When the language was not script-supported:** follow the prose Detection Algorithm in `{versionResolutionFile}` directly (Ruby / C# / Swift / etc. fall outside the script's coverage).
198	
199	Surface the result regardless of which path produced it:
200	
201	**If `target_version` was provided in step 01:**
202	- Display: "**Target version:** {target_version} (user-specified)"
203	
204	Display: "**Detected version:** {version or 'Not detected — will default to 1.0.0'}"
205	
206	{If target_version was provided AND auto-detected version differs:}
207	"**Note:** Detected version ({detected_version}) differs from your target version ({target_version}). Using target version (per `references/version-resolution.md` precedence rules)."
208	
209	If detection fails or returns a non-semver value: note that version will default to `"1.0.0"` and the user can override in step 04. The actual write happens in step 05.
210	
211	### 5. Report Analysis Summary
212	
213	Present the complete analysis:
214	
215	"**Analysis Complete**
216	
217	---
218	
219	**Target:** {repo URL or path}
220	**Language:** {detected language} ({confidence})
221	**Structure:** {file count} files across {directory count} directories
222	
223	**Key Modules ({count}):**
224	{bulleted list of modules}
225	
226	**Public Exports/Entry Points ({count}):**
227	{bulleted list of exports}
228	
229	**Notable Files:**
230	- README: {found/not found}
231	- Tests: {found/not found — location}
232	- Docs: {found/not found — location}
233	- Config: {list of config files found}
234	- Version: {detected version or "Not detected — defaulting to 1.0.0"}
235	
236	---
237	
238	{If language confidence is low:}
239	**Note:** Language detection confidence is low. You'll be able to override this in the next step.
240	
241	Moving to scope definition where you'll choose what to include and exclude."
242	
243	### 6. Auto-Proceed to Scope Definition
244	
245	Display: "**Proceeding to scope definition...**
246	
247	Review the analysis above. If anything looks wrong, let me know now — otherwise I'll proceed to scope definition."
248	
249	Pause briefly for user input. If the user provides corrections or asks questions, address them and re-present any updated analysis findings. Then proceed.
250	
251	#### Menu Handling Logic:
252	
253	- After analysis report is presented to user and any corrections addressed, load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
254	
255	#### EXECUTION RULES:
256	
257	- This is a soft auto-proceed step — present the pause prompt, wait briefly for user input
258	- If user provides corrections: address them, then proceed
259	- If no user input after a brief pause: proceed directly to step 03
260	
261	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
262	
263	ONLY WHEN the analysis is complete and the summary has been presented to the user will you load and read fully `./step-03-scope-definition.md` to begin scope definition.
264	
265	
```

9	# Step 4: Confirm Brief
10	
11	## STEP GOAL:

13	To present the complete skill brief in human-readable format, highlighting all fields that will be written to skill-brief.yaml, and obtain explicit user approval before writing.

44	- **scope.exclude:** {exclude patterns from step 03}
45	- **scope.notes:** {any scope notes from step 03}

48	- **scripts_intent:** {detect/none/description from step 03, or "detect" if not explicitly set}
49	- **assets_intent:** {detect/none/description from step 03, or "detect" if not explicitly set}
50	- **source_authority:** {official/community/internal from step 01 — default "community"}
51

57

78

82	
83	{If scripts_intent or assets_intent was explicitly set (not default "detect"):}
84	Scripts:    {scripts_intent}
85	Assets:     {assets_intent}
86	
87	Source Authority: {source_authority}
88	
89	{If target_version is set:}

91	Detected Version: {detected_version or "N/A"}
92	{Else:}
93	Version:    {version}
94	
95	Created:    {created}
96	Created by: {created_by}
97	```
98	
99	---"

101	### 4. Highlight Items Needing Attention

1	---
2	briefSchemaFile: 'assets/skill-brief-schema.md'
3	versionResolutionFile: 'references/version-resolution.md'
4	nextStepFile: './step-06-health-check.md'
5	---
6	
7	# Step 5: Write Brief
8	
9	## STEP GOAL:
10	
11	To generate the complete skill-brief.yaml from the approved brief data and write it to the forge data folder, completing the brief-skill workflow.
12	
13	## Rules
14	
15	- Focus only on writing the file — all decisions have been made
16	- Do not change any field values without user request — the brief was already approved
17	- Create the output directory if it doesn't exist
18	- Chains to the local health-check step via `{nextStepFile}` after completion — the user-facing success summary is NOT the terminal step
19	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`; written artifact (`description`, `notes`) in `{document_output_language}`
20	
21	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
22	
23	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
24	
25	### 1. Load Schema Template
26	
27	Load `{briefSchemaFile}` to reference the YAML template structure.
28	
29	### 2. Create Output Directory
30	
31	Create the directory `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/` if it doesn't already exist.
32	
33	If `{forge_data_folder}` is not set or doesn't exist:
34	- Fall back to `{output_folder}/forge-data/{skill-name}/`
35	- Inform user: "**Note:** forge_data_folder not configured. Writing to {output_folder}/forge-data/{skill-name}/ instead."
36	
37	### 2b. Existing Brief — Overwrite Policy
38	
39	Before writing, check whether `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml` already exists.
40	
41	**Interactive (`{headless_mode}` is false):**
42	
43	If the file exists, present:
44	
45	"**An existing brief was found at `{path}`.**
46	Overwrite it with the brief you just approved? [Y/N]"
47	
48	- **[Y]** Overwrite — proceed to §3.
49	- **[N]** Cancel — emit a single-line stderr log `brief-skill: overwrite-cancelled at {path}` and HALT with exit code 5 (do not chain to step-06; the run produced no new artifact).
50	
51	**Headless (`{headless_mode}` is true):**
52	
53	If the file exists:
54	
55	- If `force` was supplied as a headless argument: log `"headless: force-overwriting existing brief at {path}"` and proceed to §3.
56	- Otherwise: emit the error-variant `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope (see §4b) on stderr with `halt_reason: "overwrite-cancelled"`, exit code 5, and HALT.
57	
58	If the file does not exist, proceed normally.
59	
60	### 3. Generate skill-brief.yaml
61	
62	**Resolve the `version` field per `{versionResolutionFile}` precedence:**
63	
64	- `target_version` (set in step 01) wins outright when present.
65	- Otherwise: auto-detected version from step 02.
66	- Otherwise: `"1.0.0"`.
67	
68	The reference's invariant section is binding: when `target_version` is set, the written brief MUST satisfy `brief.target_version == brief.version`. Set both fields to the identical string. Different values are a contract violation.
69	
70	Generate the YAML file using the approved field values and the schema template:
71	
72	```yaml
73	---
74	name: "{approved skill name}"
75	version: "{resolved version — target_version if set, else detected source version, else 1.0.0}"
76	source_type: "{source or docs-only}"
77	source_repo: "{approved source repo or doc site URL}"
78	language: "{approved language}"
79	description: "{approved description}"
80	forge_tier: "{approved forge tier}"
81	created: "{current date}"
82	created_by: "{user_name}"
83	scope:
84	  type: "{approved scope type}"
85	  include:
86	    - "{approved include patterns}"
87	  exclude:
88	    - "{approved exclude patterns}"
89	  notes: "{approved scope notes or empty string}"
90	---
91	```
92	
93	**Conditional optional field inclusion:**
94	
95	**If `target_version` was set in step 01:**
96	Include the `target_version` field in the generated YAML — its value MUST be identical to the `version` field above:
97	```yaml
98	target_version: "{target_version — same value as version}"
99	```
100	
101	**If `source_type: "docs-only"` OR supplemental `doc_urls` were collected:**
102	Include the `doc_urls` array (uncommented) in the generated YAML:
103	```yaml
104	doc_urls:
105	  - url: "{documentation URL}"
106	    label: "{page label}"
107	```
108	When `source_type: "docs-only"`: `doc_urls` is required (at least one entry), `source_repo` may be set to the doc site URL for reference or omitted.
109	
110	**If `scripts_intent` was collected and is not the default `"detect"`:**
111	Include the `scripts_intent` field (uncommented):
112	```yaml
113	scripts_intent: "{none or description}"
114	```
115	
116	**If `assets_intent` was collected and is not the default `"detect"`:**
117	Include the `assets_intent` field (uncommented):
118	```yaml
119	assets_intent: "{none or description}"
120	```
121	
122	**Always include** `source_authority` (default: `"community"`, forced to `"community"` when `source_type: "docs-only"`):
123	```yaml
124	source_authority: "{official|community|internal}"
125	```
126	
127	### 4. Write the File
128	
129	Write the generated YAML to `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml`.
130	
131	If write fails:
132	- Interactive: **HALT** — "**Error:** Failed to write skill-brief.yaml. Please check that the directory is writable and try again."
133	- Headless: emit the error-variant `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope (see §4b) on stderr with `halt_reason: "write-failed"`, exit code 4, and HALT.
134	
135	### 4b. Headless Result Envelope
136	
137	If `{headless_mode}` is true, emit a single-line JSON envelope on **stdout** immediately after the successful write (before §5 / §6 / §7) so pipeline schedulers can parse the outcome without grepping the prose summary:
138	
139	```
140	SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON: {"status":"success","brief_path":"{abs_path}","skill_name":"{name}","version":"{resolved version}","language":"{language}","scope_type":"{scope.type}","exit_code":0,"halt_reason":null}
141	```
142	
143	The envelope shape on HARD HALT (any phase, written to **stderr**) uses the same keys with `status: "error"`, `brief_path: null` when no file was written, the matching `exit_code` from the table in `SKILL.md`, and `halt_reason` set to one of: `"input-missing"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`.
144	
145	When `{headless_mode}` is false, skip this section silently — no envelope is emitted.
146	
147	### 5. QMD Collection Registration (Deep Tier Only)
148	
149	**IF forge tier is Deep AND QMD tool is available:**
150	
151	Index the skill brief into a QMD collection so that portfolio-level searches can find existing briefs and avoid duplicate skill creation across large monorepos.
152	
153	**Collection creation:**
154	
155	Create a QMD collection targeting only the brief file:
156	```bash
157	qmd collection add {forge_data_folder}/{skill-name} --name {skill-name}-brief --mask "skill-brief.yaml"
158	qmd embed
159	```
160	
161	If collection already exists (re-briefing): remove and recreate for atomic replace:
162	```bash
163	qmd collection remove {skill-name}-brief
164	qmd collection add {forge_data_folder}/{skill-name} --name {skill-name}-brief --mask "skill-brief.yaml"
165	qmd embed
166	```
167	
168	**Embed verification:**
169	
170	After `qmd embed` completes, verify the collection was embedded:
171	- Run `qmd status` or `qmd collection list` and confirm `{skill-name}-brief` shows document count > 0
172	- If verification succeeds: proceed to registry update normally
173	- If verification fails: log warning "QMD embed verification failed for {skill-name}-brief — collection may not be searchable yet", still proceed to registry update but add `status: "pending"` field to the registry entry
174	
175	**Registry update:**
176	
177	Read `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml` and update the `qmd_collections` array.
178	
179	If an entry with `name: "{skill-name}-brief"` already exists, replace it. Otherwise, append:
180	
181	```yaml
182	  - name: "{skill-name}-brief"
183	    type: "brief"
184	    source_workflow: "brief-skill"
185	    skill_name: "{skill-name}"
186	    created_at: "{current ISO date}"
187	    # status: "pending"    # Added only when embed verification fails
188	```
189	
190	Write the updated forge-tier.yaml.
191	
192	**Error handling:**
193	- If QMD collection creation fails: log the error. Do NOT fail the workflow — the brief file was already written successfully.
194	- If forge-tier.yaml update fails: log the error, continue.
195	
196	**IF forge tier is NOT Deep:** Skip this section silently. No messaging.
197	
198	### 6. Display Success Summary
199	
200	"**Skill brief written successfully.**
201	
202	---
203	
204	**File:** `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml`
205	**Skill:** {name}
206	**Language:** {language}
207	**Scope:** {scope type}
208	**Forge Tier:** {forge tier}
209	
210	---
211	
212	## Next Steps
213	
214	Your skill brief is ready. To compile the actual skill from this brief, run:
215

220	- **export-skill** — Package the skill for distribution
221	
222	---

224	**Brief-skill workflow complete.**"

227

231	
232	This step chains to the local health-check step (`{nextStepFile}`), which in turn delegates to `shared/health-check.md`. After the health check completes, the brief-skill workflow is fully done.
