7	
8	# Step 3: Scope Definition
9	
10	## STEP GOAL:
11	
12	To collaboratively define the skill's inclusion and exclusion boundaries using the analysis findings from step 02, scope templates, and the user's intent from step 01.
13	
14	## Rules
15	
16	- Focus only on defining scope boundaries — do not write the brief yet (Step 05)
17	- Do not make scope decisions unilaterally — user drives all scope choices
18	- Produce: scope type, include patterns, exclude patterns
19	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`
20	- **Re-entry from step-04 [R] revise:** prior selections (`scope.type`, `scope.include`, `scope.exclude`, `scope.notes`, `scripts_intent`, `assets_intent`, supplemental `doc_urls`) are preserved as the current state. Re-present them at each section as the existing answer; the user only re-confirms or overrides. Do not reset to the §2c template menu unless the user explicitly asks to start scope over.
21	
22	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
23	
24	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
25	
26	### 1. Present Scope Context
27	
28	"**Let's define the scope for your skill.**
29	
30	Based on the analysis, here's what we're working with:
31	
32	- **Target:** {repo}
33	- **Language:** {detected language}
34	- **Modules found:** {count} — {list names}
35	- **Your intent:** {user intent from step 01}
36	{If scope hints from step 01:}
37	- **Your initial scope hints:** {hints}"
38	
39	### 2. Handle Docs-Only Mode (if applicable)
40	
41	**If `source_type: "docs-only"`:**
42	
43	"**Docs-only mode — scope is defined by documentation pages.**
44	
45	You've provided these documentation URLs:
46	{numbered list of doc_urls with labels}
47	
48	Which pages should be included in the skill? (Enter numbers, or 'all')
49	Any additional documentation URLs to add?"
50	
51	Wait for confirmation. Then skip to section 5 (Summarize Scope Decisions) with:
52	- `scope.type: "docs-only"`
53	- `scope.include`: confirmed doc URLs
54	- `scope.notes: "Generated from external documentation. All content is T3 confidence."`
55	
56	**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue to scope templates below.
57	
58	### 2b. Confirm Supplemental Documentation (if doc_urls collected)
59	
60	**If `source_type: "source"` AND supplemental `doc_urls` were collected in step 01:**
61	
62	"**Supplemental documentation URLs:**
63	{numbered list of collected doc_urls with labels}
64	
65	These will be included as T3 external references in the skill brief.
66	Add, remove, or confirm these URLs."
67	
68	Wait for confirmation. Record any changes to `doc_urls`.
69	
70	For each URL in the final list (newly added or carried over), HEAD-check it (`curl -sI {url}` or equivalent). On a 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout, warn `"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}."` and offer the same correct/keep choice as step-01 §3. The check is best-effort — never HALT on a failed HEAD — but the failure must surface here so it is not discovered downstream during compilation.
71	
72	**If no supplemental doc_urls were collected:** Skip this subsection.
73	
74	**Scope guidance for first-time users:** A well-scoped skill covers one cohesive capability with 3-8 primary functions. If the scope includes unrelated concerns (e.g., authentication AND data visualization), suggest splitting into separate briefs. If the scope is too narrow (single utility function), suggest expanding to the surrounding capability surface.
75	
76	### 2c. Offer Scope Templates
77	
78	Load `{scopeTemplatesFile}` for the scope type options ([F], [M], [P], [C], [R]) and their descriptions.
79	
80	Present: "**How broadly should this skill cover the library?**" followed by the scope type options from the loaded reference.
81	
82	Ask: "Which scope type fits your needs?"
83	
84	Wait for user selection.
85	
86	### 3. Define Boundaries Based on Selection
87	
88	Using the boundary definitions from `{scopeTemplatesFile}`, present the appropriate flow for the user's selected scope type ([F], [M], [P], [C], or [R]). Follow each type's prompts and wait for user input at each phase before proceeding.
89	
90	### 4. Handle Language Override
91	
92	{If language detection confidence was low from step 02:}
93	
94	"**Language confirmation needed.**
95	
96	The analysis detected **{language}** with low confidence. Is this correct, or should we set a different primary language?"
97	
98	Wait for confirmation or override.
99	
100	### 5. Summarize Scope Decisions
101	
102	"**Scope Summary:**
103	
104	**Type:** {Full Library / Specific Modules / Public API / Component Library / Reference App}
105	
106	**Include:**
107	{bulleted list of include patterns}
108	
109	**Exclude:**
110	{bulleted list of exclude patterns}
111	
112	**Language:** {confirmed language}
113	
114	{If any scope notes:}
115	**Notes:** {scope notes}
116	
117	Does this look right? You can adjust before we continue."
118	
119	Wait for confirmation. Make adjustments if requested.
120	
121	### 5b. Scripts & Assets Intent (Optional)
122	
123	**Only ask when `scope.type` is `full-library`, `specific-modules`, `component-library`, or `reference-app` (skip for `public-api` and `docs-only`). Reference apps routinely ship wiring scripts and build-config assets — prompt for them.**
124	
125	"Does this library include executable scripts (CLI tools, validation scripts, setup helpers) or static assets (config templates, JSON schemas, example configs) that should be packaged with the skill?"
126	
127	- **[D] Auto-detect** from source (default) — SKF will scan for `scripts/`, `bin/`, `assets/`, `templates/`, `schemas/` directories
128	- **[N] None expected** — skip script/asset detection
129	- Or describe what you expect (free text)
130	
131	Record the response as `scripts_intent` and `assets_intent` in the brief. Default to `detect` if user does not respond or skips.
132	
133	### 6. Present MENU OPTIONS
134	
135	Display: **Select an Option:** [A] Advanced Elicitation [P] Party Mode [C] Continue to Brief Confirmation
136	
137	#### Menu Handling Logic:
138	
139	- IF A: Invoke {advancedElicitationSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
140	- IF P: Invoke {partyModeSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
141	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
142	- IF Any other comments or queries: help user respond then [Redisplay Menu Options](#6-present-menu-options)
143	
144	#### EXECUTION RULES:
145	
146	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
147	- **GATE [default: C]** — If `{headless_mode}`: consume the headless inputs from step-01 in priority order:
148	  - If `scope_type` was supplied, use it (must match one of the six valid types) and skip the §2c template menu.
149	  - Otherwise auto-select based on `source_type`: `docs-only` → `scope.type: "docs-only"`; `source` → `full-library` (default).
150	  - If `include`/`exclude` were supplied, use them verbatim (split on comma) instead of running the boundary prompts in §3.
151	  - If `scripts_intent`/`assets_intent` were supplied, record them and skip §5b; otherwise default to `detect`.
152	  - Log: `"headless: scope_type={value} include={n} exclude={n} scripts_intent={value} assets_intent={value}"`.
153	- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
154	- After other menu items execution, return to this menu
155	- User can chat or ask questions — always respond and then redisplay menu
156	
157	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
158	
159	ONLY WHEN C is selected and scope boundaries are confirmed will you load and read fully `./step-04-confirm-brief.md` to present the complete brief for confirmation.
160	
161

src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-04-enrich.md:67:**Tool resolution for qmd_bridge:** QMD MCP exposes a single `query` tool that accepts a `searches[]` array. Each entry has a `type` field — `'lex'` for BM25 keyword search, `'vec'` for semantic vector search, `'hyde'` for hypothetical-document retrieval — plus `query` and `intent` fields. Claude Code: `mcp__plugin_qmd-plugin_qmd__query`. Cursor: qmd MCP server. CLI: `qmd search "{query}"` (BM25) / `qmd vector-search "{query}"` (semantic). See `knowledge/tool-resolution.md`.
src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md:43:Where `{name}` is the skill name from the brief (kebab-case) and `{version}` is the semver version from the brief (with build metadata stripped per `knowledge/version-paths.md`).
src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-brief.md:99:- Verify repository exists via `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` — **Tool resolution:** `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{branch}?recursive=1` or direct file listing if local; see `knowledge/tool-resolution.md`
src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/sub/step-02b-ccc-discover.md:47:**Tool resolution for ccc_bridge.ensure_index:** Use `/ccc` skill indexing (Claude Code), ccc MCP server (Cursor), or `cd {source_root} && ccc init` + `ccc index` (CLI). Note: `ccc init` takes no positional arguments — it initializes the index for the current working directory. See `knowledge/tool-resolution.md`.
src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/sub/step-02b-ccc-discover.md:65:**Tool resolution for ccc_bridge.search:** Use `/ccc` skill search (Claude Code), ccc MCP server (Cursor), or `cd {source_root} && ccc search --limit 20 "{query}"` (CLI). Note: `ccc search` operates on the index in the current working directory — there is no flag to specify a project directory. See `knowledge/tool-resolution.md`.
src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md:13:versionPathsKnowledge: 'knowledge/version-paths.md'
src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md:159:- **Otherwise** run `git -C "{source_path}" rev-parse HEAD` and compare to `pinned_commit`. Accept full-SHA or short-SHA-prefix match (stored pins are often 8-char short hashes — see `src/knowledge/provenance-tracking.md`).
src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md:197:   - **Tool resolution:** Use `/ccc` skill search (Claude Code), ccc MCP server (Cursor), or CLI. Note: `ccc search` operates on the index in the current working directory. See `knowledge/tool-resolution.md`.
src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md:249:**Tool resolution for gh_bridge:** Use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{branch}?recursive=1` for list_tree, `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}` for read_file. If source is local, use direct file listing/reading instead. See `knowledge/tool-resolution.md`.
src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md:265:**Tool resolution for ast_bridge:** Use ast-grep MCP tools (`mcp__ast-grep__find_code`, `mcp__ast-grep__find_code_by_rule`) as specified in the AST Extraction Protocol above, or `ast-grep` CLI. For `detect_co_imports`, use `find_code_by_rule` with a co-import YAML rule scoped to the libraries list. See `knowledge/tool-resolution.md`.
```

Gather Target Repository\n\n\"**What repository or documentation do you want to create a skill for?**\n\nProvide one of:\n- A **GitHub URL** (e.g., `https://github.com/org/repo`)\n- A **local path** (e.g., `/path/to/project`)\n- **Documentation URLs** for a docs-only skill (e.g., `https://docs.stripe.com/api`) — use this when no source code is available (SaaS, closed-source)\n\n**Target:**\"\n\nWait for user response.\n\n**If user provides documentation URLs (not a repo):**\n- Set `source_type: \"docs-only\"` in the brief data\n- Collect one or more doc URLs with optional labels\n- For each collected URL, perform a `HEAD` request (or `curl -sI`) to verify the URL resolves with a 2xx/3xx status:\n  - On 2xx/3xx: silently accept.\n  - On 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout: warn `\"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}. Confirm the URL is correct, or proceed anyway.\"` Interactive: re-prompt for a corrected URL or `[K] Keep anyway`. Headless: keep the URL and log the warning — the brief still records it but the failure is now visible at brief-creation time instead of materializing hours later in skf-create-skill.\n- Note: `source_authority` will be forced to `community` (T3 external documentation)\n- Note: `source_repo` becomes optional (can be set to the main doc site URL for reference)\n\n**If user provides a GitHub URL or local path:**\n- Set `source_type: \"source\"` (default)\n- Optionally ask: \"Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context? (These will be fetched as T3 external references.)\"\n- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`\n\n**Source authority (for all source-type skills):**\n\"**Are you the maintainer of this library, or creating a community skill?**\"\n- If maintainer: set `source_authority: \"official\"`\n- If community user: set `source_authority: \"community\"` (default)\n- If internal/proprietary: set `source_authority: \"internal\"`\n\nDefault to `\"community\"` if user does not specify or skips. For `docs-only` skills, `source_authority` is always forced to `\"community\"`.\n\nConfirm the target.",
  "new_string": "### 3. Gather Target Repository\n\nThis section has three sub-flows. Execute exactly one branch — 3.2 *or* 3.3 — based on the user's response in 3.1, then end with the shared confirmation. Do not mix branches.\n\n#### 3.1 Collect target\n\n\"**What repository or documentation do you want to create a skill for?**\n\nProvide one of:\n- A **GitHub URL** (e.g., `https://github.com/org/repo`)\n- A **local path** (e.g., `/path/to/project`)\n- **Documentation URLs** for a docs-only skill (e.g., `https://docs.stripe.com/api`) — use this when no source code is available (SaaS, closed-source)\n\n**Target:**\"\n\nWait for user response. Branch on the response:\n\n- Documentation URLs only (no source location) → §3.2\n- GitHub URL or local filesystem path → §3.3\n\n#### 3.2 Branch — Documentation URLs (docs-only)\n\n- Set `source_type: \"docs-only\"` in the brief data\n- Collect one or more doc URLs with optional labels\n- For each collected URL, perform a `HEAD` request (or `curl -sI`) to verify the URL resolves with a 2xx/3xx status:\n  - On 2xx/3xx: silently accept.\n  - On 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout: warn `\"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}. Confirm the URL is correct, or proceed anyway.\"` Interactive: re-prompt for a corrected URL or `[K] Keep anyway`. Headless: keep the URL and log the warning — the brief still records it but the failure is now visible at brief-creation time instead of materializing hours later in skf-create-skill.\n- Set `source_authority: \"community\"` (forced for docs-only — T3 external documentation; the §3.3 source-authority prompt is skipped)\n- Note: `source_repo` becomes optional (can be set to the main doc site URL for reference)\n\nSkip §3.3 and continue at \"Confirm the target\" below.\n\n#### 3.3 Branch — Source (GitHub URL or local path)\n\n- Set `source_type: \"source\"` (default)\n- Optionally ask: \"Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context? (These will be fetched as T3 external references.)\"\n- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`\n\n**Source authority (this branch only — docs-only forces `community` in §3.2):**\n\n\"**Are you the maintainer of this library, or creating a community skill?**\"\n- If maintainer: set `source_authority: \"official\"`\n- If community user: set `source_authority: \"community\"` (default)\n- If internal/proprietary: set `source_authority: \"internal\"`\n\nDefault to `\"community\"` if user does not specify or skips.\n\n---\n\nConfirm the target."
}

  "old_string": "### 4. List Top-Level Modules and Exports\n\nIdentify the public API surface. **Delegate the parsing to `{extractPublicApiScript}` whenever the detected language is supported** — the script is the single source of truth for manifest parsing, export discovery, and version detection across the whole SKF pipeline. Hand-rolling these in prose creates drift seams the LLM cannot fully close.\n\n**Script-supported languages** (use the script): `js`, `ts`, `javascript`, `typescript`, `python`, `rust`, `go`, `java`, `kotlin`.\n\n**Procedure when supported:**\n\n1. Read the relevant files into memory (no parsing yet — just collect content). For GitHub sources use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{file}` with base64 decode; for local sources read directly.\n\n   | Language | Manifest | Entry points (mode=quick) |\n   |----------|----------|--------------------------|\n   | js / ts / javascript / typescript | `package.json` (root, or primary workspace package per `references/version-resolution.md`) | `index.{ts,js}` and/or `src/index.{ts,js}` if present |\n   | python | `pyproject.toml` (or `setup.py` / `setup.cfg` if no `pyproject.toml`) | top-level `__init__.py` of the package, plus `_version.py` if present |\n   | rust | `Cargo.toml` (`[package]` — workspace root if `version = { workspace = true }`) | `src/lib.rs` |\n   | go | `go.mod` | top-level `*.go` exporting the package surface |\n   | java | `pom.xml` | (manifest alone is sufficient for the modules listing) |\n   | kotlin | `build.gradle` / `build.gradle.kts` | (manifest alone) |\n\n2. Build a JSON payload matching the script contract:\n\n   ```json\n   {\n     \"language\": \"<one of the supported values>\",\n     \"manifest\": {\"path\": \"<relative path>\", \"content\": \"<file contents>\"},\n     \"entries\":  [{\"path\": \"<relative path>\", \"content\": \"<file contents>\"}, ...],\n     \"mode\":     \"quick\"\n   }\n   ```\n\n3. Invoke the script and parse its JSON stdout:\n\n   ```bash\n   echo '<payload-json>' | uv run {extractPublicApiScript} --language <lang> --mode quick\n   ```\n\n   On a non-zero exit (codes 1 or 2 per the script's docstring), capture stderr, log it, and fall through to the prose-fallback path below — never HALT just because the script choked on an unusual manifest.\n\n4. Render the returned `package_name`, `exports` (each entry's `name`/`type`/`source_file`), `dependencies`, and any `warnings` to the user. The script also returns `version` — feed that into §4b instead of re-deriving.\n\n5. The script does not enumerate directories under `src/`. The LLM still lists those as \"Top-Level Modules/Directories\" so the user sees structural context (Maven and Gradle are the exception — for those, the script returns a `modules` array which IS the list).\n\n**Procedure when not supported** (Ruby / C# / Swift / etc.):\n\nFall back to ad-hoc inspection — `Gemfile` / `*.csproj` / `*.sln` / `Package.swift` / file extension frequency. List top-level source directories as potential modules and note any obvious entry points. Flag the limitation in the analysis summary so the user knows scoping is on coarser signals.\n\n**Output format (both paths):**\n\n\"**Top-Level Modules/Directories:**\n{numbered list of modules with brief description of each}\n\n**Detected Exports/Entry Points:**\n{numbered list of public-facing items found — from script output when available, ad-hoc inspection otherwise}\"\n\n**Semantic Signals (Forge+ and Deep with ccc only):**\n\n**Remote source guard:** If the target source was resolved via GitHub API (remote URL, not a local file path), skip this CCC subsection — CCC requires a local source index and cannot operate on remote-only sources. Note: \"CCC semantic discovery skipped — target is remote. CCC discovery will run automatically during create-skill after the source is cloned.\"\n\nIf `tools.ccc` is true in forge-tier.yaml, supplement the module listing with a semantic discovery pass:\n\n**CCC Semantic Discovery:**\n- **Claude Code:** Use `/ccc search \"{repo_name} public API exports modules\" {source_path}`\n- **Cursor:** Use `ccc` MCP server `search` tool with query `\"{repo_name} public API exports modules\"` and path `{source_path}`\n- **CLI fallback:** `ccc search \"{repo_name} public API exports modules\" --path {source_path} --limit 10`\n\nSee `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` for full bridge-to-tool mapping.\n\nIf results are returned, display:\n\n\"**Semantic Signals (ccc):**\n{numbered list of file:snippet pairs from CCC results — top 5 most relevant}\"\n\nThis supplements — never replaces — the explicit module list above. CCC may surface non-obvious entry points (dynamically constructed exports, re-export chains) that static directory analysis misses.\n\nIf CCC is unavailable or returns no results: skip this subsection silently.",
  "new_string": "### 4. List Top-Level Modules and Exports\n\nIdentify the public API surface. **Delegate the parsing to `{extractPublicApiScript}` whenever the detected language is supported** — the script is the single source of truth for manifest parsing, export discovery, and version detection across the whole SKF pipeline. Hand-rolling these in prose creates drift seams the LLM cannot fully close.\n\n**Script-supported languages** (use the script): `js`, `ts`, `javascript`, `typescript`, `python`, `rust`, `go`, `java`, `kotlin`.\n\nThis section runs exactly one of §4.1 (script path) or §4.2 (fallback path) based on the detected language, then always emits §4.3 (output format) and conditionally §4.4 (semantic signals).\n\n#### 4.1 Procedure — script-supported languages\n\n1. Read the relevant files into memory (no parsing yet — just collect content). For GitHub sources use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{file}` with base64 decode; for local sources read directly.\n\n   | Language | Manifest | Entry points (mode=quick) |\n   |----------|----------|--------------------------|\n   | js / ts / javascript / typescript | `package.json` (root, or primary workspace package per `references/version-resolution.md`) | `index.{ts,js}` and/or `src/index.{ts,js}` if present |\n   | python | `pyproject.toml` (or `setup.py` / `setup.cfg` if no `pyproject.toml`) | top-level `__init__.py` of the package, plus `_version.py` if present |\n   | rust | `Cargo.toml` (`[package]` — workspace root if `version = { workspace = true }`) | `src/lib.rs` |\n   | go | `go.mod` | top-level `*.go` exporting the package surface |\n   | java | `pom.xml` | (manifest alone is sufficient for the modules listing) |\n   | kotlin | `build.gradle` / `build.gradle.kts` | (manifest alone) |\n\n2. Build a JSON payload matching the script contract:\n\n   ```json\n   {\n     \"language\": \"<one of the supported values>\",\n     \"manifest\": {\"path\": \"<relative path>\", \"content\": \"<file contents>\"},\n     \"entries\":  [{\"path\": \"<relative path>\", \"content\": \"<file contents>\"}, ...],\n     \"mode\":     \"quick\"\n   }\n   ```\n\n3. Invoke the script and parse its JSON stdout:\n\n   ```bash\n   echo '<payload-json>' | uv run {extractPublicApiScript} --language <lang> --mode quick\n   ```\n\n   On a non-zero exit (codes 1 or 2 per the script's docstring), capture stderr, log it, and fall through to §4.2 (the prose-fallback path) — never HALT just because the script choked on an unusual manifest.\n\n4. Render the returned `package_name`, `exports` (each entry's `name`/`type`/`source_file`), `dependencies`, and any `warnings` to the user. The script also returns `version` — feed that into §4b instead of re-deriving.\n\n5. The script does not enumerate directories under `src/`. The LLM still lists those as \"Top-Level Modules/Directories\" so the user sees structural context (Maven and Gradle are the exception — for those, the script returns a `modules` array which IS the list).\n\n#### 4.2 Procedure — fallback (not script-supported)\n\nLanguages outside the script coverage (Ruby / C# / Swift / etc.) take this path. The §4.1 fall-through on script error also lands here.\n\nFall back to ad-hoc inspection — `Gemfile` / `*.csproj` / `*.sln` / `Package.swift` / file extension frequency. List top-level source directories as potential modules and note any obvious entry points. Flag the limitation in the analysis summary so the user knows scoping is on coarser signals.\n\n#### 4.3 Output format (both paths)\n\n\"**Top-Level Modules/Directories:**\n{numbered list of modules with brief description of each}\n\n**Detected Exports/Entry Points:**\n{numbered list of public-facing items found — from script output when available, ad-hoc inspection otherwise}\"\n\n#### 4.4 Semantic Signals (Forge+/Deep with ccc only)\n\n**Remote source guard:** If the target source was resolved via GitHub API (remote URL, not a local file path), skip this CCC subsection — CCC requires a local source index and cannot operate on remote-only sources. Note: \"CCC semantic discovery skipped — target is remote. CCC discovery will run automatically during create-skill after the source is cloned.\"\n\nIf `tools.ccc` is true in forge-tier.yaml, supplement the module listing with a semantic discovery pass:\n\n**CCC Semantic Discovery:**\n- **Claude Code:** Use `/ccc search \"{repo_name} public API exports modules\" {source_path}`\n- **Cursor:** Use `ccc` MCP server `search` tool with query `\"{repo_name} public API exports modules\"` and path `{source_path}`\n- **CLI fallback:** `ccc search \"{repo_name} public API exports modules\" --path {source_path} --limit 10`\n\nSee `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` for full bridge-to-tool mapping.\n\nIf results are returned, display:\n\n\"**Semantic Signals (ccc):**\n{numbered list of file:snippet pairs from CCC results — top 5 most relevant}\"\n\nThis supplements — never replaces — the explicit module list above. CCC may surface non-obvious entry points (dynamically constructed exports, re-export chains) that static directory analysis misses.\n\nIf CCC is unavailable or returns no results: skip this subsection silently."
}
```

1	# Scope Templates Reference
2	
3	## Scope Type Options
4	
5	Present these options to the user for selection:
6	
7	**[F] Full Library** — Include everything. Best for smaller, focused libraries.
8	- All public exports, all modules
9	- Exclude only tests, build artifacts, and internal utilities
10	
11	**[M] Specific Modules** — Select which modules to include. Best for large libraries where only some parts are relevant.
12	- You choose which modules/directories
13	- Fine-grained control over what's in and out
14	
15	**[P] Public API Only** — Include only the public-facing API surface. Best for libraries with a clear public/private boundary.
16	- Entry points and exported interfaces only
17	- Internal implementation excluded
18	
19	**[C] Component Library** — Optimized for UI component libraries with registries, props-based APIs, and design system variants.
20	- Component registry as primary API surface (not individual exports)
21	- Props interfaces as API contracts (not function signatures)
22	- Auto-exclude demo/example/story files (with user confirmation)
23	- Variant consolidation across design systems
24	
25	**[R] Reference App** — Whole-app pattern-reference skill. Use when the source is a working example app and the skill's value is **wiring patterns** (lifecycle, IPC, build-config, distribution) rather than a public library API.
26	- Pattern surface as primary API slot (not individual exports)
27	- Adoption Steps as primary workflow format (not API-call chains)
28	- Tier 2 organized as `references/pattern-*.md` groupings (not per-function)
29	- Export-count stats are pattern-surface proxies, not library exports
30	
31	## Boundary Definitions by Scope Type
32	
33	### Full Library Boundaries
34	
35	Default inclusions:
36	- All source files under {main source directory}
37	- All public modules: {list from analysis}
38	
39	Default exclusions:
40	- Test files (`**/*.test.*`, `**/*.spec.*`, `**/test/`, `**/tests/`)
41	- Build artifacts (`**/dist/`, `**/build/`, `**/target/`)
42	- Configuration files
43	- Documentation source files
44	
45	Prompt: "Any additional exclusions you'd like to add? Or adjustments to these defaults?"
46	
47	### Specific Modules Boundaries
48	
49	**Phase 1 — Module selection:**
50	
51	Present numbered list of modules from step 02 with brief descriptions.
52	Prompt: "Which modules would you like to include? (Enter numbers, comma-separated):"
53	
54	**Phase 2 — Granularity within selected modules:**
55	
56	For selected modules, ask:
57	- **A)** Everything in those modules (all files)
58	- **B)** Only public exports from those modules
59	
60	Prompt: "Any files or patterns to explicitly exclude within these modules?"
61	
62	### Public API Only Boundaries
63	
64	**Phase 1 — Export selection:**
65	
66	Present numbered list of exports/entry points from step 02.
67	Prompt: "Which of these would you like to include? (Enter numbers, or 'all'):"
68	
69	**Phase 2 — Confirm exclusions:**
70	
71	Exclusions will include all internal implementation files, tests, and utilities.
72	Prompt: "Any additional items you'd like to include or exclude?"
73	
74	### Reference App Boundaries
75	
76	**Phase 1 — Pattern surface intent:**
77	
78	Ask: "What is the authored pattern surface for this skill? List the files (or directories) the user must touch to adopt the pattern — entry points, config files, lifecycle hooks, build scripts."
79	
80	- Record the user's list as `scope.tier_a_include` when narrower than a broad `scope.include`. Reference-app briefs benefit strongly from `tier_a_include` because the denominator is small and precise.
81	- Prompt follow-up: "Any files outside that list that should still be in scope for completeness (tests, fixtures, supporting configs)?"
82	
83	**Phase 2 — Scope.include and exclusions:**
84	
85	Set `scope.include` to the pattern-surface file list (or broader union when the author flagged supporting files). Default exclusions mirror the Full Library defaults (tests, build artifacts, docs source). Record `scope.notes` with a one-sentence description of the pattern (e.g., "Embedded Python sidecar pattern for Electron apps — lifecycle orchestration, RPC proxy, build-copy wiring").
86	
87	**Phase 3 — Confirmation:**
88	
89	Summary showing: pattern surface count, `tier_a_include` vs `include` distinction, notes. Prompt: "Does this reference-app scope look right? Adjust before continuing."
90	
91	### Docs-Only Boundaries
92	
93	**No source code access.** Scope is defined by the `doc_urls` collected during intent gathering.
94	
95	- All content derived from external documentation
96	- No include/exclude patterns — coverage determined by fetched documentation
97	- All extractions labeled T3 (`[EXT:{url}]` citations)
98	
99	Prompt: "Any additional documentation URLs to include? Or URLs to exclude from the ones collected?"
100	
101	### Component Library Boundaries
102	
103	**Phase 1 — Registry Detection:**
104	
105	Auto-detect or accept explicit `registry_path` from user. Scan source tree for files matching common registry patterns:
106	- Files named `registry.ts`, `components.ts`, `index.ts` in `registry/`, `catalog/`, or `components/` directories
107	- Arrays of objects with `{ id, name, component }` structure and 10+ entries
108	- Files with `Component[]` type annotations
109	
110	Present detected registry candidate(s) to user for confirmation.
111	Prompt: "I found what looks like a component registry at {path} ({count} entries). Is this correct? Or provide the registry path:"
112	
113	**Phase 2 — Demo/Example Exclusion:**
114	
115	Auto-detect demo directories and file patterns:
116	- Directories: `demo/`, `demos/`, `stories/`, `examples/`, `__stories__/`, `storybook/`
117	- Files: `*.stories.*`, `*.story.*`, `*.example.*`, `*.demo.*`
118	
119	Present detected patterns to user before applying (do NOT exclude silently).
120	Prompt: "**Auto-detected {N} demo/example files** in {M} directories. Confirm exclusion? [Y/n] Or adjust patterns:"
121	
122	**Phase 3 — Variant Selection (if applicable):**
123	
124	If multiple design system variant directories detected (e.g., `react-shadcn/`, `react-baseui/`, `react-carbon/`):
125	- Present detected variants with component counts per variant
126	- User selects primary variant and which variants to include
127	- Record as `ui_variants` in brief
128	
129	Prompt: "I detected {count} design system variants: {list with counts}. Which is the primary variant? Include all? [Y/n]"
130	
131	**Phase 4 — Scope Confirmation:**
132	
133	Summary showing: component count, excluded demo count, variant summary, include/exclude patterns.
134	Prompt: "Does this component library scope look right? Adjust before continuing."
135	
136	## Scripts & Assets Detection (Optional Refinement)
137	
138	When `scripts_intent` or `assets_intent` is `detect` (default), SKF auto-detects from source directories matching: `scripts/`, `bin/`, `tools/`, `cli/` (for scripts) and `assets/`, `templates/`, `schemas/`, `configs/`, `examples/` (for assets). Detection applies to all scope types except `docs-only`.

Empty input or just Enter accepts the recommendation; any of the five letters overrides.\n\n**Headless:** if `scope_type` was supplied, use it (consumed at the GATE in §6). Pattern (adjust naturally — do not template-stamp):\n\n```\n{What the skill enables an agent to do, in active voice}, drawn from {target}{ at version {target_version}, if set}{ for {scope hint summary}, if any scope hints were captured}. Use when {one-sentence trigger condition derived from intent — what task or question routes to this skill}.\n```",
  "new_string": "Compose a candidate 1-3 sentence description from the gathered material. **Write like a human library maintainer would** — what does an agent get from this skill, and when should it route here? Two facts must come through (what the skill is, when to use it); everything else is voice. Resist filling in the same skeleton every time.\n\nExamples of the range — note that voice, structure, lead, and emphasis all vary:\n\n> Render Markdown to HTML using the marked library. Use when the user pastes raw Markdown and wants formatted output, or asks how to convert MD files in a build pipeline.\n\n> Stripe API client for Node.js — payment intents, subscriptions, customer portal, webhooks. Triggers on tasks involving Stripe-managed payments, subscription billing, or webhook event handling.\n\n> Charts and visualizations powered by D3.js. Reach for this when the user asks to plot data, build interactive graphs, or wants bare D3 control instead of a React-charts abstraction.\n\n> Lint Python code with Ruff. Use when the user wants to add or configure Ruff in a Python project, debug rule selectors, or understand why a specific check fired.\n\n> Date and time arithmetic via Luxon — parsing, formatting, time zones, durations, intervals. Use when working with dates in ways that exceed `Date.toISOString()` but you don't want a full Moment.js footprint.\n\nNotice how each one leads differently (verb / noun / \"Charts and...\" / verb / noun-phrase) and how the trigger (\"Use when...\", \"Triggers on...\", \"Reach for this when...\") is matched to the voice rather than copy-pasted. Compose in that spirit using the gathered material — the target repo, the user's intent, the version if set, and any scope hints — but do not template-stamp."
}
```

### Theme 4 Commit 1: Parallel HEAD batching for doc_urls

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "- For each collected URL, perform a `HEAD` request (or `curl -sI`) to verify the URL resolves with a 2xx/3xx status:\n  - On 2xx/3xx: silently accept.\n  - On 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout: warn `\"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}. Confirm the URL is correct, or proceed anyway.\"` Interactive: re-prompt for a corrected URL or `[K] Keep anyway`. Headless: keep the URL and log the warning — the brief still records it but the failure is now visible at brief-creation time instead of materializing hours later in skf-create-skill.",
  "new_string": "- HEAD-check the collected URLs in parallel — do not loop sequentially. Issue all N `curl -sI {url}` (or equivalent) calls in a **single message with N parallel Bash calls**, then process the responses together. Each call must use a 5-second timeout (`curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`) to bound worst-case wall-time on hung hosts. Per response:\n  - On 2xx/3xx: silently accept.\n  - On 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout: warn `\"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}. Confirm the URL is correct, or proceed anyway.\"` Interactive: re-prompt for a corrected URL or `[K] Keep anyway`. Headless: keep the URL and log the warning — the brief still records it but the failure is now visible at brief-creation time instead of materializing hours later in skf-create-skill."
}

Each unnecessary load costs context (~5-10 KB per reference) and biases the LLM toward consulting material the current path does not need.\n- Always communicate in `{communication_language}`\n- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with their default action and log each auto-decision"

"""SKF Validate Frontmatter — agentskills.io frontmatter compliance checker.

Validates SKILL.md frontmatter against the agentskills.io specification.
Aligned with the canonical reference implementation at
agentskills/agentskills/skills-ref/src/skills_ref/validator.py.

Designed for pre-check use in test-skill, create-skill, update-skill, and
export-skill workflows. Returns structured JSON for deterministic integration.

CLI — invoke via `uv run` so the PEP 723 PyYAML dependency declared
above is auto-resolved on first call and cached. `docs/getting-started.md`
documents uv as the runtime prerequisite for exactly this. Bare
`python3` will fail with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'yaml'`
on a fresh interpreter where pyyaml has not been pip-installed
system-wide:

  uv run skf-validate-frontmatter.py <skill-md-path>
  uv run skf-validate-frontmatter.py <skill-md-path> --skill-dir-name <name>

Input:
  Path to a SKILL.md file.
  Optional --skill-dir-name: expected directory name for name-match check.
  If omitted, derived from the parent directory of the SKILL.md path.

Output:
  JSON object:
    status:     "pass" | "fail" | "warn"
    issues:     list of { severity, field, message }
    frontmatter: parsed frontmatter dict (if parseable)
    summary:    { total, high, medium, low }

Exit codes:
  0  — pass (no high-severity issues)
  1  — fail (high-severity issues found) or error
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import sys
import unicodedata
from pathlib import Path

import yaml

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Tests for skf-extract-public-api.py.

The helper does no I/O — it parses content passed in via JSON. Tests
build payloads inline, call extract() directly, and assert on the
shape of the returned envelope. Per-language manifest parsers and
export scanners are also exercised individually.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import importlib.util
import io
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
    "skf_extract_public_api",
    Path(__file__).parent.parent / "src" / "shared" / "scripts" / "skf-extract-public-api.py",
)
mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
spec.loader.exec_module(mod)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# JS / TS
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


class TestPackageJson:
    def test_basic(self):
        out = mod.parse_package_json('{"name":"foo","version":"1.0","description":"d","dependencies":{"a":"1","b":"2"}}')
        assert out["name"] == "foo"
        assert out["version"] == "1.0"
        assert out["description"] == "d"
        assert out["dependencies"] == ["a", "b"]

    def test_malformed_records_warning(self):
        out = mod.parse_package_json("{ this is not json")
        assert "_parse_error" in out
        assert "JSON parse error" in out["_parse_error"]

    def test_root_must_be_object(self):
        out = mod.parse_package_json('["array", "not", "object"]')
        assert "_parse_error" in out


class TestJsExportScanner:
    def test_decl_forms(self):
        src = (
            "export const VERSION = 1;\n"
            "export function helloFn() {}\n"
            "export class FooClass {}\n"
            "export interface Bar {}\n"
            "export type Quux = string;\n"
            "export enum State { On }\n"
            "export default function defaulted() {}\n"
        )
        names = [e["name"] for e in mod.scan_exports_js(src, "src/index.ts")]
        assert names == ["VERSION", "helloFn", "FooClass", "Bar", "Quux", "State", "defaulted"]

    def test_re_exports_with_alias(self):
        src = 'export { Quux as Pub, Other } from "./internal";\n'
        names = [e["name"] for e in mod.scan_exports_js(src, "src/index.ts")]
        assert names == ["Pub", "Other"]

    def test_dedup_across_decl_and_reexport(self):
        # If both forms surface the same name, we only keep one entry.
        src = "export const Foo = 1;\nexport { Foo } from './other';\n"
        names = [e["name"] for e in mod.scan_exports_js(src, "src/index.ts")]
        assert names == ["Foo"]


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Python
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-validate-brief-inputs.py",
  "content": "# /// script\n# requires-python = \">=3.9\"\n# dependencies = []\n# ///\n\"\"\"SKF Validate Brief Inputs — pre-pass validation for brief-skill headless invocation.\n\nValidates and normalizes the headless argument set passed to skf-brief-skill\nbefore the workflow's interactive sequence runs. Catches malformed inputs at\npoint of capture instead of letting them surface 5+ minutes later in step-02\nor step-05.\n\nCLI:\n  uv run skf-validate-brief-inputs.py --json '{...}'\n  echo '{...}' | uv run skf-validate-brief-inputs.py\n\nInput (JSON object on stdin or via --json):\n  Required:\n    target_repo  — string (URL or path); error if absent\n    skill_name   — string (kebab-case); error if absent or malformed\n\n  Optional with enum constraints:\n    source_type      — \"source\" | \"docs-only\" (default \"source\")\n    source_authority — \"official\" | \"community\" | \"internal\" (default \"community\")\n    scope_type       — \"full-library\" | \"specific-modules\" | \"public-api\"\n                       | \"component-library\" | \"reference-app\" | \"docs-only\"\n    scripts_intent   — \"detect\" | \"none\" | free-text (default \"detect\")\n    assets_intent    — \"detect\" | \"none\" | free-text (default \"detect\")\n\n  Optional with format constraints:\n    target_version — loose semver string (matches 1.2.3, 1.2.3-rc.1, 1.2.3+build.5)\n\n  Conditional:\n    doc_urls — required when source_type == \"docs-only\"\n\n  Free-text / pass-through:\n    scope_hint, language_hint, intent, include, exclude, force\n\n  Unrecognized keys are passed through unchanged but flagged as warnings.\n\nOutput (JSON on stdout):\n  {\n    \"valid\": bool,\n    \"errors\":   [{\"field\": \"...\", \"message\": \"...\"}, ...],\n    \"warnings\": [{\"field\": \"...\", \"message\": \"...\"}, ...],\n    \"normalized\": { ...input dict with defaults applied... },\n    \"halt_reason\": \"input-missing\" | \"input-invalid\" | null\n  }\n\nExit codes:\n  0 — valid (errors empty)\n  1 — invalid (errors present); halt_reason is set\n  2 — internal error (bad JSON input, IO failure)\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport re\nimport sys\nfrom typing import Any\n\nKNOWN_FIELDS = {\n    \"target_repo\",\n    \"skill_name\",\n    \"source_type\",\n    \"source_authority\",\n    \"scope_type\",\n    \"target_version\",\n    \"doc_urls\",\n    \"scope_hint\",\n    \"language_hint\",\n    \"intent\",\n    \"scripts_intent\",\n    \"assets_intent\",\n    \"include\",\n    \"exclude\",\n    \"force\",\n}\n\nVALID_SOURCE_TYPES = {\"source\", \"docs-only\"}\nVALID_SOURCE_AUTHORITIES = {\"official\", \"community\", \"internal\"}\nVALID_SCOPE_TYPES = {\n    \"full-library\",\n    \"specific-modules\",\n    \"public-api\",\n    \"component-library\",\n    \"reference-app\",\n    \"docs-only\",\n}\n\nKEBAB_RE = re.compile(r\"^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?$\")\nSEMVER_RE = re.compile(\n    r\"^(\\d+)(\\.\\d+){0,2}([.\\-+][0-9A-Za-z][0-9A-Za-z.\\-+]*)?$\"\n)\nURL_RE = re.compile(r\"^https?://\", re.IGNORECASE)\n\n\ndef _err(field: str, message: str) -> dict[str, str]:\n    return {\"field\": field, \"message\": message}\n\n\ndef validate(inp: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    errors: list[dict[str, str]] = []\n    warnings: list[dict[str, str]] = []\n\n    # Required: target_repo, skill_name\n    target_repo = inp.get(\"target_repo\")\n    skill_name = inp.get(\"skill_name\")\n    if not target_repo:\n        errors.append(_err(\"target_repo\", \"missing required argument target_repo\"))\n    if not skill_name:\n        errors.append(_err(\"skill_name\", \"missing required argument skill_name\"))\n\n    # skill_name format\n    if skill_name and isinstance(skill_name, str) and not KEBAB_RE.match(skill_name):\n        errors.append(\n            _err(\n                \"skill_name\",\n                f\"skill_name must be kebab-case (lowercase letters/digits/hyphens, \"\n                f\"no leading or trailing hyphen). Got: {skill_name!r}\",\n            )\n        )\n\n    # source_type enum\n    source_type_raw = inp.get(\"source_type\", \"source\")\n    if source_type_raw not in VALID_SOURCE_TYPES:\n        errors.append(\n            _err(\n                \"source_type\",\n                f\"source_type must be one of {sorted(VALID_SOURCE_TYPES)}. Got: {source_type_raw!r}\",\n            )\n        )\n        # Treat as default for downstream conditional logic\n        source_type = \"source\"\n    else:\n        source_type = source_type_raw\n\n    # source_authority enum\n    source_authority_raw = inp.get(\"source_authority\", \"community\")\n    if source_authority_raw not in VALID_SOURCE_AUTHORITIES:\n        errors.append(\n            _err(\n                \"source_authority\",\n                f\"source_authority must be one of {sorted(VALID_SOURCE_AUTHORITIES)}. \"\n                f\"Got: {source_authority_raw!r}\",\n            )\n        )\n\n    # scope_type enum (when present)\n    scope_type = inp.get(\"scope_type\")\n    if scope_type is not None and scope_type not in VALID_SCOPE_TYPES:\n        errors.append(\n            _err(\n                \"scope_type\",\n                f\"scope_type must be one of {sorted(VALID_SCOPE_TYPES)}. Got: {scope_type!r}\",\n            )\n        )\n\n    # target_version semver-ish\n    target_version = inp.get(\"target_version\")\n    if target_version is not None:\n        if not isinstance(target_version, str):\n            errors.append(\n                _err(\n                    \"target_version\",\n                    f\"target_version must be a string. Got type {type(target_version).__name__}\",\n                )\n            )\n        elif not SEMVER_RE.match(target_version.lstrip(\"v\")):\n            errors.append(\n                _err(\n                    \"target_version\",\n                    f\"target_version does not look like semver. Got: {target_version!r}. \"\n                    f\"Expected forms: 1.2.3, 1.2.3-rc.1, 1.2.3+build.5\",\n                )\n            )\n\n    # docs-only requires doc_urls\n    doc_urls = inp.get(\"doc_urls\")\n    if source_type == \"docs-only\" and not doc_urls:\n        errors.append(\n            _err(\"doc_urls\", \"doc_urls is required when source_type is docs-only\")\n        )\n\n    # target_repo shape (warning only — script doesn't HEAD-check)\n    if isinstance(target_repo, str) and target_repo:\n        looks_like_url = URL_RE.match(target_repo) is not None\n        looks_like_path = (\n            target_repo.startswith(\"/\")\n            or target_repo.startswith(\"./\")\n            or target_repo.startswith(\"~\")\n        )\n        if not (looks_like_url or looks_like_path):\n            warnings.append(\n                _err(\n                    \"target_repo\",\n                    f\"target_repo does not look like a URL or absolute/relative path: {target_repo!r}\",\n                )\n            )\n\n    # Unknown fields → warn\n    for key in inp:\n        if key not in KNOWN_FIELDS:\n            warnings.append(_err(key, f\"unrecognized field {key!r} — passed through unchanged\"))\n\n    # Build normalized payload\n    normalized: dict[str, Any] = dict(inp)\n    normalized.setdefault(\"source_type\", \"source\")\n    normalized.setdefault(\"source_authority\", \"community\")\n    normalized.setdefault(\"scripts_intent\", \"detect\")\n    normalized.setdefault(\"assets_intent\", \"detect\")\n\n    # Force community when docs-only\n    if normalized.get(\"source_type\") == \"docs-only\":\n        if normalized.get(\"source_authority\") not in (None, \"community\"):\n            warnings.append(\n                _err(\n                    \"source_authority\",\n                    f\"source_authority forced to 'community' because source_type=docs-only \"\n                    f\"(was {normalized['source_authority']!r})\",\n                )\n            )\n        normalized[\"source_authority\"] = \"community\"\n\n    # halt_reason classification\n    if errors:\n        missing_required = any(\n            e[\"message\"].startswith(\"missing required\") or \"is required\" in e[\"message\"]\n            for e in errors\n        )\n        halt_reason = \"input-missing\" if missing_required else \"input-invalid\"\n    else:\n        halt_reason = None\n\n    return {\n        \"valid\": not errors,\n        \"errors\": errors,\n        \"warnings\": warnings,\n        \"normalized\": normalized,\n        \"halt_reason\": halt_reason,\n    }\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"skf-validate-brief-inputs\")\n    p.add_argument(\n        \"--json\",\n        help=\"JSON object as a string. If omitted, the script reads JSON from stdin.\",\n    )\n    args = p.parse_args()\n\n    raw = args.json\n    if raw is None:\n        raw = sys.stdin.read()\n\n    if not raw or not raw.strip():\n        sys.stderr.write(\"skf-validate-brief-inputs: empty input\\n\")\n        return 2\n\n    try:\n        inp = json.loads(raw)\n    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:\n        sys.stderr.write(f\"skf-validate-brief-inputs: invalid JSON input: {e}\\n\")\n        return 2\n\n    if not isinstance(inp, dict):\n        sys.stderr.write(\"skf-validate-brief-inputs: input must be a JSON object\\n\")\n        return 2\n\n    result = validate(inp)\n    json.dump(result, sys.stdout, indent=2, sort_keys=True)\n    sys.stdout.write(\"\\n\")\n    return 0 if result[\"valid\"] else 1\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main())\n"
}

  "new_string": "KEBAB_RE = re.compile(r\"^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?$\")\n# Require full X.Y.Z form (with optional v prefix and pre-release/build suffix).\n# Loose forms like `1`, `1.2`, `v2` are rejected — the user should write the\n# explicit triple. CalVer (e.g. 2024.04.01) is accepted because it satisfies\n# the X.Y.Z shape.\nSEMVER_RE = re.compile(\n    r\"^v?\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+([.\\-+][0-9A-Za-z][0-9A-Za-z.\\-+]*)?$\"\n)\nURL_RE = re.compile(r\"^https?://\", re.IGNORECASE)"

(These will be fetched as T3 external references.)\"\n- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`",
  "new_string": "**If user provides a GitHub URL or local path:**\n- Set `source_type: \"source\"` (default)\n- **Pre-validate the target before continuing — fail fast at point of capture, not 5+ minutes later in step-02.** Issue these probes in a single message with parallel Bash calls:\n  - **GitHub URL:** `curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`. On a 4xx (typically 404 for a typo'd repo or org), warn `\"GitHub returned {status} for {url} — confirm the URL is correct.\"` and re-prompt. On 2xx, accept. (The full `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` check still runs in step-02 §1 to catch private-repo access issues — this HEAD probe is just for typo catch.)\n  - **GitHub URL, in parallel with the above:** `gh auth status` — if it reports unauthenticated or the binary is missing, warn `\"GitHub CLI not authenticated; step-02 will HALT when it tries to fetch the tree. Run 'gh auth login' before continuing, or supply a local clone path instead.\"` (Do not HALT here — let the user choose to fix or proceed; the canonical HALT still happens in step-02 §1's failure-class triage.)\n  - **Local path:** verify the directory exists (`test -d {path}`). If not, warn `\"Local path {path} does not exist.\"` and re-prompt.\n- Optionally ask: \"Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context? (These will be fetched as T3 external references.)\"\n- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md",
  "old_string": "**If user provides a version:** Store as `target_version`. Set `version` to this value.\n**If blank:** Proceed without `target_version` — version will be auto-detected in step 02.",
  "new_string": "**If user provides a version:** Validate the shape against `^v?\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+([.\\-+][0-9A-Za-z][0-9A-Za-z.\\-+]*)?$` (full X.Y.Z form, with optional `v` prefix and pre-release / build suffix; CalVer like `2024.04.01` accepted; partial forms like `1`, `1.2`, `v2`, `latest` rejected). On a match, store as `target_version` and set `version` to this value. On a non-match, warn `\"'{value}' doesn't look like semver — write the explicit triple (e.g. 1.0.0). Fix it now or skip auto-detection?\"` and re-prompt for a corrected value or blank to fall through to step-02 auto-detection. Catching this here prevents the step-05 invariant check (`brief.target_version == brief.version` per `references/version-resolution.md`) from blowing up after the user has already approved the brief.\n**If blank:** Proceed without `target_version` — version will be auto-detected in step 02."
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md",
  "old_string": "**Collision check (interactive and headless):** before locking the name, check whether `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/skill-brief.yaml` already exists. If it does:\n\n- Interactive: \"**Heads up — a brief for `{name}` already exists at `{path}`.** Pick a different name to keep the new brief separate, or confirm to continue (the existing brief's overwrite prompt fires in step 05).\"\n- Headless: log `\"warn: skill name '{name}' collides with existing brief at {path}\"` and proceed; the existing-brief overwrite policy in step-05 §2b is the canonical gate (HALT with `overwrite-cancelled` unless `force` was supplied).",
  "new_string": "**Collision check (interactive and headless):** before locking the name, check whether `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/skill-brief.yaml` already exists. If it does:\n\n- Interactive: generate three candidate alternates by scanning sibling directories under `{forge_data_folder}/`:\n  1. `{name}-v{N}` where `N` is the smallest positive integer that doesn't collide (e.g. `{name}-v2`, `{name}-v3`)\n  2. `{name}-{target_version}` if `target_version` is set and the suffix wouldn't collide (e.g. `marked-1.2.3`)\n  3. `{name}-{source_authority}` if not `community` (e.g. `marked-internal` for an internal fork)\n\n  Then present:\n\n  ```\n  **Heads up — a brief for `{name}` already exists at `{path}`.**\n\n  Suggested alternates (none collide):\n    [1] {alternate-1}\n    [2] {alternate-2}\n    [3] {alternate-3}\n\n  Pick a number to use that name, type a different name, or press Enter to keep `{name}` and let step-05 §2b handle the overwrite prompt.\n  ```\n\n  On a numbered choice, replace `{name}` with the chosen alternate. On Enter, fall through to step-05's overwrite gate. On any other input, treat as a new candidate name and re-run the collision check against it.\n\n- Headless: log `\"warn: skill name '{name}' collides with existing brief at {path}\"` and proceed; the existing-brief overwrite policy in step-05 §2b is the canonical gate (HALT with `overwrite-cancelled` unless `force` was supplied)."
}

Now I have all findings. Let me compile the review.

---

Reviewing the 7 commits on `feat/skf-brief-skill-fail-fast-validation` against `main`. Files examined:

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/test/test-skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md`
- All other workflow SKILL.md files (create-skill, update-skill, audit-skill, setup, test-skill) for consumer impact

---

## CRITICAL

**None found.** No other SKILL.md consumer branches on `halt_reason` values; the new `input-invalid` value is isolated to skf-brief-skill's headless pipeline. The validator's logic is sound for the primary paths.

---

## IMPORTANT

### 1. Documentation drift: `"input-invalid"` missing from step-05 §4b's `halt_reason` enum (Confidence: 95)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md`, line 143

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md`, line 71

SKILL.md's Result Contract correctly enumerates `"input-invalid"` in the `halt_reason` union. Step-05 §4b has a separate inline copy of that list describing the envelope shape on HARD HALT, and it reads:

> `halt_reason` set to one of: `"input-missing"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`.

`"input-invalid"` is absent. Since step-01 is the step that HALTs with `exit_code: 2` when the validator returns `valid: false`, and `input-invalid` is one of two `valid: false` outcomes (per step-01 §8 and SKILL.md's exit-codes table), a headless pipeline author reading step-05 §4b as the canonical envelope spec would not know to handle that value. The GATE in step-01 also references the exit-code-2 halt with `halt_reason: "input-invalid"` explicitly, so the omission in step-05 is a genuine drift.

**Fix:** Add `"input-invalid"` to the step-05 §4b `halt_reason` list, making it consistent with SKILL.md line 71 and step-01 §8:

```
`halt_reason` set to one of: `"input-missing"`, `"input-invalid"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`.
```

---

### 2. Missing test: mixed required-missing + format-invalid does not assert `halt_reason` priority (Confidence: 85)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/test/test-skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`, `TestRequiredFields.test_both_missing_emits_two_errors` (line 55)

The classification logic in the script (lines 232-237) uses `"input-missing"` when any error matches the "missing required" string pattern, even when other errors are simultaneously format violations. The priority is intentional and correct, but there is no test that verifies it. Specifically:

- `test_both_missing_emits_two_errors` asserts that both field errors are present, but does not assert `halt_reason == "input-missing"`.
- No test covers the case where a required field is absent AND a separate field has an enum/format error simultaneously (e.g., `{"skill_name": "foo", "scope_type": "bad-value"}` — missing `target_repo` plus invalid `scope_type`). In that case `halt_reason` should be `"input-missing"`, which is what the code produces, but nothing proves it.

This matters because the GATE in step-01 §8 branches on `halt_reason` to decide which error category to surface to the operator log. If the classification logic were accidentally changed (e.g., using `all` instead of `any`, or reordering the priority check), tests would not catch it.

**Fix:** Add two test cases to `TestRequiredFields`:

```python
def test_both_missing_halt_reason_is_input_missing(self):
    out = mod.validate({})
    assert out["halt_reason"] == "input-missing"

def test_missing_required_plus_invalid_enum_prefers_input_missing(self):
    # target_repo absent + scope_type invalid — "missing" should dominate
    out = mod.validate({"skill_name": "foo", "scope_type": "not-real"})
    assert out["valid"] is False
    assert out["halt_reason"] == "input-missing"
```

---

### 3. SEMVER_RE accepts `1.2.3-` (trailing separator, empty label) — inconsistency with docstring (Confidence: 82)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`, line 97-99

The regex:

```python
r"^v?\d+\.\d+\.\d+([.\-+][0-9A-Za-z][0-9A-Za-z.\-+]*)?$"
```

The suffix group requires `[.\-+]` followed by at least one `[0-9A-Za-z]`. This correctly rejects `1.2.3-` (nothing after the separator). However, it accepts `1.2.3-a` (single-char pre-release label), which is fine semantically.

The actual problem is with inputs like `1.2.3-0` — a zero pre-release label. The regex accepts it, which is correct per semver. No issue there.

The actual edge case that is a real bug: `1.2.3` with a suffix that begins with a digit separator, e.g. `1.2.3.0` is accepted (matches as CalVer-style fourth component), but the step-01 docstring says CalVer is accepted for the `X.Y.Z` shape — and `1.2.3.0` satisfies that via the suffix. This is **intentional** based on the CalVer note. Confidence drops.

After closer analysis: the regex has a subtle issue with inputs like `1.2.3-rc.1` where the separator after `.1` is processed correctly. The real gap is that `1.2.3+` (trailing `+`) would fail because the suffix group requires `[.\-+]` then `[0-9A-Za-z]` with no second char being optional — but `+` alone at the end has nothing after it, so the group wouldn't match the `+` either. `1.2.3+` correctly fails.

Reducing to confirmed regex edge case: inputs of the form `1.2.3-RC` (uppercase) are accepted because the suffix character class includes uppercase `A-Z`. Step-01 says the form should be `1.2.3-rc.1` (lowercase shown in example) but the regex is more permissive. The test suite doesn't include an uppercase pre-release label. This is not a bug per se since the docstring says "pre-release/build suffix" without a case constraint, but it means `1.2.3-RC.1` is valid while `1` is not. This is acceptable semver behavior. Confidence in this being a real bug drops below 80.

Withdrawing finding 3 — the regex behavior is acceptable and consistent with the stated intent.

---

## Summary of Actionable Findings

**2 real issues found**, both IMPORTANT severity:

**Finding 1 (Confidence: 95)** — Documentation drift in step-05 §4b: `"input-invalid"` is absent from the `halt_reason` enumeration in the HARD HALT envelope description, while SKILL.md's Result Contract includes it. This is a direct consequence of the PR adding `input-invalid` to SKILL.md without propagating it to the step-05 copy. A headless pipeline author reading step-05 §4b as the spec would not know to handle `input-invalid` and might misclassify the exit. Fix: add `"input-invalid"` to line 143 of `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md`.

**Finding 2 (Confidence: 85)** — The test that verifies both-fields-missing (`test_both_missing_emits_two_errors`) doesn't assert `halt_reason`, and there is no test for the priority rule when missing-required and format-invalid errors co-occur. The classification logic is correct but unguarded by tests. Fix: add two assertions to `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/test/test-skf-validate-brief-inputs.py` as shown above.

**Consumer impact:** None. No other workflow (create-skill, update-skill, audit-skill, setup, test-skill) consumes `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` or branches on the `halt_reason` field. Adding `input-invalid` is a purely additive change to the public contract with zero breaking risk.

**Correctness of the Python script:** The validation logic, default-application order, docs-only conditional, and halt_reason classification are all correct for the primary paths. The `normalized` dict correctly applies defaults via `setdefault` after copying `inp`, which means supplied values win over defaults without any ordering hazard.

# /// script
# requires-python = ">=3.10"
# dependencies = []
# ///
"""SKF Emit Result Envelope — Schema-locked headless output for skf-setup.

Replaces the prose-driven envelope assembly in `src/skf-setup/steps-c/
step-04-report.md` §4 with one Python invocation. The envelope contract
(SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON) was added to step-04 in PR #247, extended in
PR #248 (header / outputs / failure modes), and extended again in this
PR (previous_tier, tier_changed, tools_added, tools_removed, error).

LLM-rendered envelopes risk silent schema drift on every invocation —
a pipeline that grep's `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON: {…}` out of the workflow
log can break if the model decides to rename a key or rearrange a
nested structure. This script is the single source of truth: it takes
a context payload as JSON on stdin, computes derived fields
deterministically, and emits the envelope in a fixed shape that
matches the JSON Schema at
`src/shared/scripts/schemas/skf-setup-result-envelope.v1.json`.

Subcommands:

  emit       Read context payload as JSON on stdin, derive missing
             fields (tools_added/removed, tier_changed), assemble the
             envelope, emit `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON: {one-line JSON}`
             on stdout. Default subcommand.

  validate   Read an envelope (without the prefix) as JSON on stdin
             and verify it against the documented schema. No stdout
             on success; non-zero exit + stderr error on failure.
             Useful for paranoid pipelines that want to validate a
             received envelope before consuming it.

Context payload shape (consumed by `emit`):

  {
    "tier":                          "Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep",
    "previous_tier":                 "Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep|null",
    "tools":                         {"ast_grep": bool, "gh_cli": bool, "qmd": bool, "ccc": bool},
    "previous_tools":                {…same shape…|null},
    "config_path":                   "/abs/path/to/forge-tier.yaml",
    "ccc_index":                     {"status": "...", "indexed_path": "...|null", "file_count": int|null},
    "files_written":                 ["forge-tier.yaml", ...],
    "tier_override_active":          bool,
    "tier_override_invalid":         bool,
    "tier_override_invalid_value":   "string|null",
    "tier_override_invalid_suggestion": "string|null",
    "tier_override_unsafe":          bool,
    "tier_override_unsafe_missing":  ["gh", "qmd", ...],
    "require_tier_satisfied":        bool|null,
    "require_tier_failure_missing":  ["ccc", ...],
    "qmd_status":                    "absent|daemon_stopped|healthy",
    "ccc_exclusion_warnings":        ["string", ...],
    "ccc_registry_stale_removed":    ["/path", ...],
    "ccc_indexing_failed_reason":    "string|null",
    "error":                         null|{"phase","path","reason"}
  }

Caller does NOT need to compute warnings, tools_added/removed, or
===forge-tier===
# /// script
# requires-python = ">=3.10"
# dependencies = ["pyyaml"]
# ///
"""SKF Forge Tier RW — Read/write primitives for forger-sidecar YAML files.

Replaces the prose-driven YAML emission in `src/skf-setup/steps-c/
step-02-write-config.md` §1-§3 (and the prose-driven cleanup logic in
step-03 §5/§5b) with one Python invocation. The script is the source
of truth for the on-disk forge-tier.yaml schema (matching the
canonical template at step-02-write-config.md:24-58) and guarantees
that registry arrays are PRESERVED across rewrites — losing
`qmd_collections` or `ccc_index_registry` would break every
downstream skill (skf-create-skill, skf-audit-skill, skf-update-skill,
skf-brief-skill) that reads them.

Subcommands:

  read          Read forge-tier.yaml and emit the parsed structure as JSON
                on stdout. Missing-file is not an error — emits null
                payload with status=ok so first-run callers can branch.

  write-tools   Write a fresh forge-tier.yaml from a JSON context payload
                on stdin. Preserves `qmd_collections`,
                `ccc_index_registry`, and the user-customizable
                `ccc_index.staleness_threshold_hours` from the existing
                file (if any) by reading it first, then merging.

  init-prefs    Create preferences.yaml with first-run defaults IF it
                does not exist. Idempotent — refuses to overwrite an
                existing file (preserves user customization).

  clean-stale   Two cleanup operations gated by flags:
                  --qmd-live-names a,b,c — remove qmd_collections
                    entries whose `name` is not in the comma-separated
                    live-names list. Caller computes liveness via
                    `qmd collection list` (or skf-qmd-classify-
                    collections.py once that ships in PR B).
                  --prune-missing-ccc-paths — remove ccc_index_registry
                    entries whose `path` no longer exists on disk.
                Both flags can be passed in the same invocation.

Output schema (the read subcommand and the response from every write):

  {"status": "ok", "version": "v1", ...subcommand-specific fields...}

Errors emit `{"status": "error", "message": "..."}` to stderr and
exit non-zero (1 for user error, 2 for I/O failure).

Cross-platform: pure stdlib + PyYAML. Atomic writes via temp + rename
mirror skf-atomic-write.py's pattern. Concurrent access requires
external `flock` coordination — see step-07 of skf-create-skill for
the precedent.

CLI — invoke via `uv run` so the PEP 723 PyYAML dependency declared
above is auto-resolved on first call and cached. `docs/getting-started.md`
documents uv as the runtime prerequisite for exactly this. Bare
`python3` will fail with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'yaml'`
on a fresh interpreter where pyyaml has not been pip-installed
system-wide:
```

75	from datetime import datetime, timezone

78	import yaml

80	
81	DEFAULT_STALENESS_HOURS = 24
82	PREFERENCES_TEMPLATE = """# Ferris Sidecar: User Preferences
83	# Created by setup workflow on first run
84	# Edit this file to customize Ferris behavior
85	
86	# Override detected tier (set to Quick, Forge, Forge+, or Deep to force a tier)
87	tier_override: ~
88	
89	# Passive context injection (set to false to skip snippet generation and CLAUDE.md updates during export)
90	passive_context: true
91	
92	# Headless mode (set to true to skip confirmation gates in all workflows)
93	headless_mode: false
94	
95	# Compact greeting (set to true to skip the full capabilities table on session start)
96	compact_greeting: false
97	
98	# Reserved for future use — these fields are not yet consumed by any workflow step
99	# output_language: ~
100	# skill_format_version: ~
101	# citation_style: ~
102	# confidence_display: ~
103	"""
104	
105	
106	def _die(code: int, message: str) -> None:
107	    print(json.dumps({"status": "error", "message": message}), file=sys.stderr)
108	    sys.exit(code)
109	
110	
111	def _ok(payload: dict) -> None:
112	    payload.setdefault("status", "ok")
113	    payload.setdefault("version", "v1")
114	    print(json.dumps(payload, default=str))
115	
116	
117	def _read_yaml(path: Path) -> dict | None:
118	    """Return parsed YAML as dict, or None if file missing. Raises on parse error."""
119	    if not path.exists():
120	        return None
121	    try:
122	        with path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
123	            data = yaml.safe_load(f)
124	    except yaml.YAMLError as e:
125	        _die(2, f"failed to parse {path}: {e}")
126	    if data is None:
127	        return {}
128	    if not isinstance(data, dict):
129	        _die(2, f"expected mapping at top of {path}, got {type(data).__name__}")
130	    return data
131	
132	
133	def _atomic_write(target: Path, content: str) -> None:
134	    """Crash-safe write via temp + fsync + rename. Mirrors skf-atomic-write.py."""
135	    target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
136	    tmp = target.with_name(target.name + ".skf-tmp")
137	    try:
138	        fd = os.open(tmp, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC, 0o644)
139	        try:
140	            os.write(fd, content.encode("utf-8"))
141	            os.fsync(fd)
142	        finally:
143	            os.close(fd)
144	        os.replace(tmp, target)
145	    except OSError as e:
146	        if tmp.exists():
147	            try:
148	                tmp.unlink()
149	            except OSError:
150	                pass
151	        _die(2, f"atomic write failed for {target}: {e}")
152	
153	
154	def _yaml_block(value, indent: int = 0) -> str:
155	    """Dump a value as a YAML fragment, indented by `indent` spaces, no trailing newline."""
156	    text = yaml.safe_dump(value, default_flow_style=False, sort_keys=False, allow_unicode=True)
157	    text = text.rstrip("\n")
158	    if indent == 0:
159	        return text
160	    pad = " " * indent
161	    return "\n".join(pad + line if line else line for line in text.split("\n"))
162	
163	
164	def render_forge_tier_yaml(payload: dict) -> str:
165	    """Render the canonical forge-tier.yaml from a context payload.
166	
167	    Preserves the human-readable section comments from the template at
168	    step-02-write-config.md:24-58. Sections are emitted in a fixed order
169	    so re-runs against unchanged inputs produce byte-identical output.
170	    """
171	    tools = payload["tools"]
172	    tier = payload["tier"]
173	    tier_detected_at = payload["tier_detected_at"]
174	    ccc_index = payload["ccc_index"]
175	    ccc_index_registry = payload.get("ccc_index_registry", [])
176	    qmd_collections = payload.get("qmd_collections", [])
177	
178	    # Render `tools` block in the canonical key order (matches step-02 template).
179	    tools_ordered = {
180	        "ast_grep": tools["ast_grep"],
181	        "gh_cli": tools["gh_cli"],
182	        "qmd": tools["qmd"],
183	        "ccc": tools["ccc"],
184	        "ccc_daemon": tools.get("ccc_daemon"),
185	        "security_scan": tools.get("security_scan", False),
186	    }
187	
188	    # ccc_index keys in canonical order.
189	    ccc_ordered = {
190	        "indexed_path": ccc_index.get("indexed_path"),
191	        "last_indexed": ccc_index.get("last_indexed"),
192	        "status": ccc_index.get("status"),
193	        "staleness_threshold_hours": ccc_index.get("staleness_threshold_hours", DEFAULT_STALENESS_HOURS),
194	        "file_count": ccc_index.get("file_count"),
195	        "exclude_patterns": ccc_index.get("exclude_patterns", []),
196	    }
197	
198	    parts = [
199	        "# Ferris Sidecar: Forge Tier State",
200	        "# Written by setup workflow",
201	        "",
202	        "# Tool availability (detected during [SF] Setup Forge)",
203	        _yaml_block({"tools": tools_ordered}),
204	        "",
205	        "# Capability tier (derived from tool availability)",
206	        "# Quick = no tools | Forge = + ast-grep | Forge+ = + ast-grep + ccc | Deep = + ast-grep + gh + QMD",
207	        f"tier: {tier}",
208	        f"tier_detected_at: {_yaml_scalar(tier_detected_at)}",
209	        "",
210	        "# CCC semantic index state (managed by setup step-01b and extraction workflows)",
211	        _yaml_block({"ccc_index": ccc_ordered}),
212	        "",
213	        "# CCC index registry (tracks which source paths have been indexed for skill workflows)",
214	        "# PRESERVE existing entries on re-runs",
215	        _yaml_block({"ccc_index_registry": ccc_index_registry}),
216	        "",
217	        "# QMD collection registry (populated by create-skill, consumed by audit/update-skill)",
218	        "# PRESERVE existing entries on re-runs",
219	        _yaml_block({"qmd_collections": qmd_collections}),
220	        "",
221	    ]
222	    return "\n".join(parts)
223	
224	
225	def _yaml_scalar(value) -> str:
226	    """Render a scalar value using YAML's own quoting rules so timestamps, booleans, etc. round-trip."""
227	    return yaml.safe_dump(value, default_flow_style=False).rstrip("\n").rstrip("...").rstrip()
228	
229	
230	def _merge_preserved_fields(payload: dict, existing: dict | None) -> dict:
231	    """Inject preserved fields from the existing file into the new payload.
232	
233	    Three preservation rules (per step-02 §1 "Note on re-runs"):
234	    - `qmd_collections` array — preserved entirely from existing.
235	    - `ccc_index_registry` array — preserved entirely from existing.
236	    - `ccc_index.staleness_threshold_hours` scalar — preserved if user set
237	      a non-default value; else uses payload value or DEFAULT.
238	    Note: `ccc_index.exclude_patterns` is NOT preserved (rewritten fresh
239	    by step-01b on every run, per the explicit step-02 contract).
240	    """
241	    if existing is None:
242	        return payload
243	
244	    payload.setdefault("qmd_collections", existing.get("qmd_collections", []))
245	    payload.setdefault("ccc_index_registry", existing.get("ccc_index_registry", []))
246	
247	    payload.setdefault("ccc_index", {})
248	    existing_ccc = existing.get("ccc_index", {}) or {}
249	    if "staleness_threshold_hours" not in payload["ccc_index"]:
250	        payload["ccc_index"]["staleness_threshold_hours"] = existing_ccc.get(
251	            "staleness_threshold_hours", DEFAULT_STALENESS_HOURS
252	        )
253	    return payload
254
