## CRITICAL

### 1. `source_authority` unconditionally emitted — backwards compat break for existing briefs (Confidence: 90)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-write-skill-brief.py`, line 299.

`assemble_brief` always appends `source_authority` to the output dict regardless of whether the caller supplied it:

```python
# Always include source_authority
brief["source_authority"] = source_authority
```

The existing YAML template at `src/skf-brief-skill/assets/skill-brief-schema.md` treats `source_authority` as a commented-out optional field (`# source_authority: community    # Optional`). Any brief written by the old prose path that did not include `source_authority` will now have it injected with value `community`. The consequence is:

- A re-brief of an existing skill will produce a different file hash, which is not itself a bug.
- The real hazard: if a pipeline diffs old-vs-new briefs to detect changes, the `source_authority: community` insertion will appear as a change on every re-run, even when nothing else changed. This is noise at best and a false-drift signal at worst.
- More concretely, if `skf-update-skill` or `skf-audit-skill` reads an existing brief written by the prose path and then writes it back through this script (e.g., on an update), the key appears for the first time and any tooling that does a literal YAML diff against the original will report drift.

The schema and `skill-brief-schema.md` both declare `source_authority` optional. The script should emit it conditionally — only when the value is non-default (i.e., not `community`) or when the caller explicitly supplied it. Alternatively, document explicitly that the new canonical format always includes the field and update the schema description to "always present, defaults to community." Right now there is a discrepancy between the schema saying optional and the script always writing it.

**Fix:** Change the "Always include" block to only emit when non-default:

```python
if source_authority != "community":
    brief["source_authority"] = source_authority
```

Or, if the intent is to always emit it (a valid design choice), update `skill-brief-schema.md` to remove the optional designation and mark it required.

---

### 2. SKILL.md halt_reason enum does not include `input-invalid` — drift that matters for the headless consumer (Confidence: 85)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md`, line 71.

SKILL.md's Result Contract section lists the valid `halt_reason` values:

```
`halt_reason` is one of: `null` (success), `"input-missing"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`
```

`input-invalid` is absent from this enumeration. However:

- `skf-emit-brief-result-envelope.py` (line 77) accepts and maps `input-invalid` to exit code 2.
- `skf-brief-result-envelope.v1.json` (the JSON Schema used to validate envelopes at runtime) includes `input-invalid` in its enum.
- Step-05 §3 explicitly instructs the LLM to invoke the envelope script with `halt_reason: "input-invalid"` on write-script exit-1.

So the runtime machinery is internally consistent, but SKILL.md — the external contract document that headless consumers are supposed to read before integrating — is missing the case. A headless pipeline author reading SKILL.md today will not know that `input-invalid` can appear in the envelope, and may not branch on it. This is not a compile-time error; it is an integration-contract gap that will surprise consumers after PR #275 merges and envelopes start carrying `halt_reason: "input-invalid"`.

You asked whether this is "a merge conflict to call out or fine because PR #275 will land first." The answer is: this PR already teaches step-05 to emit `input-invalid` (via the envelope script's mapping), and the envelope script + JSON Schema already include it. Only SKILL.md lags. If #275 merges first, SKILL.md in `main` picks it up and the merge is clean. If this PR merges first, SKILL.md's contract section is wrong from merge until #275 lands. That window is a real, if transient, drift. Call it out so the merge order is explicit.

**Fix (if this PR merges independently of #275):** Add `"input-invalid"` to the `halt_reason` list in SKILL.md's Result Contract section and the Exit Codes table (row for code 2, alongside `input-missing`).

---

## IMPORTANT

### 3. `resolve_version` treats empty string as falsy — silent fallthrough to `detected_version` (Confidence: 85)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-write-skill-brief.py`, lines 134-140.

```python
def resolve_version(ctx: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    if ctx.get("version_resolved"):
        return ctx["version_resolved"]
    if ctx.get("target_version"):
        return ctx["target_version"]
    if ctx.get("detected_version"):
        return ctx["detected_version"]
    return "1.0.0"
```

All three guards use truthiness (`if ctx.get(field)`), which means an empty string `""` for any of these fields silently falls through to the next level instead of being caught as a malformed input. This is most likely to bite on `version_resolved`: if a caller explicitly supplies `"version_resolved": ""` (e.g., a bug in the step-05 JSON assembly), the script silently ignores it and picks up `target_version` instead, potentially producing a different version than the caller intended and no error.

The same pattern in `validate_context` at lines 232 and 240 does correctly guard with `if tv is not None` and `if detected is not None` — the inconsistency makes `resolve_version` the odd one out.

`version_resolved` is described in the docstring as "caller already ran the precedence rule" — it should be a hard error if supplied as an empty string, not a silent fallthrough.

**Fix:**

```python
def resolve_version(ctx: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    vr = ctx.get("version_resolved")
    if vr is not None:
        return vr  # empty string will then fail SEMVER_RE check in cmd_write
    tv = ctx.get("target_version")
    if tv is not None:
        return tv
    dv = ctx.get("detected_version")
    if dv is not None:
        return dv
    return "1.0.0"
```

This pushes the empty-string case to the post-`resolve_version` SEMVER_RE check in `cmd_write` (line 359–364), which will then produce a clear validation error rather than a silent version substitution.

---

### 4. Step-05 §3 headless HALT branches reference `exit code 2` and `exit code 4`, but the caller is expected to shell-exit with those codes after invoking the envelope script — the step text does not make explicit who calls `exit` (Confidence: 80)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md`, lines 109–114.

The §3 failure branches read:

```
- Exit 1 (validation/invariant): ...
  - Headless: invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript}` with `halt_reason: "input-invalid"`, exit code 2.
- Exit 2 (I/O failure): ...
  - Headless: invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript}` with `halt_reason: "write-failed"`, exit code 4.
```

The phrase "exit code 2" / "exit code 4" is ambiguous: it is not clear whether this is telling the LLM to pass `exit_code: 2` in the JSON payload fed to the envelope script, or to `exit 2` from the shell after the script call. In fact the envelope script derives exit_code from halt_reason deterministically — the JSON payload must NOT contain an `exit_code` field (it's computed, not supplied). If the LLM misreads this as a field to include in the stdin JSON, it will pass an unexpected key and the script will silently ignore it (the script uses `ctx.get("status")` etc. and never reads `exit_code` from the context).

Compare with the precedent set by step-02-analyze-target.md, which writes `halt_reason: "gh-auth-failed"` in the exit instruction with no trailing "exit code N" annotation. The step-05 wording is subtly different and could cause a model to construct a broken JSON payload.

The ambiguity also extends to §2b's headless `overwrite-cancelled` branch, which reads "then HALT with exit code 5" — this one is clearer because it follows the envelope invocation as a separate sentence.

**Fix:** Reword the §3 branches to separate the envelope call from the shell exit explicitly:

```
- Headless: invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript}` with `halt_reason: "input-invalid"` (script emits exit_code 2 in the envelope automatically), then `exit 2`.
```

This mirrors how the skf-forge-tier-rw.py docstring describes exit codes ("Errors emit `{...}` to stderr and exit non-zero (1 for user error, 2 for I/O failure)") and removes the ambiguity about where exit_code 2/4 originates.

---

### 5. `register-qmd-collection` round-trips through `render_forge_tier_yaml` without preserving unknown top-level keys (Confidence: 80)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-forge-tier-rw.py`, lines 348–364.

`cmd_register_qmd_collection` reads the existing forge-tier.yaml, updates the `qmd_collections` array, then constructs a new `payload` dict with only the keys it knows about:

```python
payload = {
    "tools": data.get("tools", {}),
    "tier": data.get("tier", "Quick"),
    "tier_detected_at": data.get("tier_detected_at", ...),
    "ccc_index": data.get("ccc_index", {}),
    "ccc_index_registry": data.get("ccc_index_registry", []),
    "qmd_collections": collections,
}
rendered = render_forge_tier_yaml(payload)
```

`render_forge_tier_yaml` only knows how to emit the six hard-coded sections. If a future version of forge-tier.yaml adds a top-level key that is not in those six (e.g. a `workspace_state` section added in a future PR), `register-qmd-collection` will silently drop it. The same pattern exists in `cmd_clean_stale` (lines 409–418).

This is not a new problem introduced by this PR — `cmd_write_tools` and `cmd_clean_stale` already have the same limitation. But `cmd_register_qmd_collection` is new in this PR and repeats the pattern rather than addressing it, so it is worth flagging explicitly.

The risk is low today (no unknown keys exist in the schema), but the step-05 §5 docstring says "preserves all other forge-tier state" — the preservation guarantee is only true for the six known sections.

**Fix:** No immediate change required for correctness today; the existing schema matches exactly. Document the limitation in the `cmd_register_qmd_collection` docstring or inline comment so future contributors adding a new forge-tier.yaml key know to update `render_forge_tier_yaml` as well.

---

|---|----------|----------|-------|
| 1 | CRITICAL | `skf-write-skill-brief.py:299` | `source_authority` always emitted; breaks round-trip for existing briefs written without it |
| 2 | CRITICAL | `SKILL.md:71` | `input-invalid` absent from Result Contract enum; merge-order dependency with #275 must be explicit |
| 3 | IMPORTANT | `skf-write-skill-brief.py:134-140` | `resolve_version` uses truthiness — empty string `version_resolved` silently falls through |
| 4 | IMPORTANT | `step-05-write-brief.md:109-114` | "exit code 2/4" annotation in §3 HALT branches ambiguous — LLM may misread as JSON payload field |
| 5 | IMPORTANT | `skf-forge-tier-rw.py:348-364` | `register-qmd-collection` drops unknown top-level keys; "preserves all other state" claim overstates |

Issues 1 and 2 need to be resolved before merge. Issues 3 and 4 are correctness bugs but lower-urgency. Issue 5 is a documentation/forward-compatibility note.

I now have everything needed to answer all five questions definitively. Here is the review.

---

Reviewing 5 open PRs (#272-#276) against `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge` main.

## Question 1: `input-invalid` in SKILL.md — Real Conflict?

Both PRs add `input-invalid` to the halt_reason enum and Exit Codes table, but the descriptions diverge.

**PR #275 Exit Code 2 row:**
> `input-missing / input-invalid` — step-01 GATE — required headless arg absent ... ⇒ `input-missing`; enum violation, malformed semver `target_version`, or non-kebab `skill_name` ⇒ `input-invalid`

**PR #276 Exit Code 2 row (superseding):**
> `input-missing / input-invalid` — step-01 GATE — required headless arg absent ... ⇒ `input-missing`; enum violation, malformed semver, non-kebab `skill_name`, **or step-05 brief-context schema validation failure** ⇒ `input-invalid`

**Finding (HIGH): Real merge conflict, not byte-identical.**

PR #276 expands the `input-invalid` trigger to include step-05 schema failures, because `skf-write-skill-brief.py` exits 1 on schema violation. The PR #275 version of the row, which lands first in the merge sequence, will be overwritten by #276's broader definition. This is an intended semantic expansion, not an error — but if #276 is merged without resolving against #275's text, git will surface a conflict on that line. Resolution: keep the PR #276 wording verbatim, as it is the superset.

## Question 2: Step-01 Multi-PR Collision (272 → 273 → 274 → 275)

File touched: `src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`

- PR #272: Adds §3 subsections, §8 GATE table — structural additions
- PR #273: Modifies §2 (forge-tier gloss), §7b (description voice examples) — different sections
- PR #274: Changes §1 URL validation to parallel curl — different section from #272/#273
- PR #275: Adds `validateBriefInputsScript` frontmatter path + wires GATE to the script — touches frontmatter block and GATE section (§8)

**Finding (HIGH): Order-sensitive collision between #272 and #275.**

PR #272 rewrites §3 and §8 (the GATE table) from prose to structured sub-sections. PR #275 adds a new frontmatter key and modifies the GATE section to wire the validation script. Both PRs modify the GATE area. If #272 merges first, the GATE section has a new table structure; #275's diff was written against the original prose form. The patch will fail to apply cleanly to the restructured table. This requires #275 to be rebased on top of #272 before merge, or the GATE section in #275's diff manually reconciled. PRs #273 and #274 edit disjoint sections and will rebase cleanly.

## Question 3: Consumer Regression on source_authority Omission and Trailing `---`

The consumers (`skf-create-skill`, `skf-update-skill`, `skf-audit-skill`) are LLM-executed markdown workflows — their step-01 files contain natural-language instructions, not Python parser calls. No Python script in the current repo reads `skill-brief.yaml` directly. `skf-validate-output.py` validates `metadata.json` (a different artifact; `source_authority` there remains unconditional). `skf-write-skill-brief.py` explicitly documents that consumers default to `"community"` when the field is absent, and the no-trailing-`---` design was chosen specifically to avoid a second YAML document for `yaml.safe_load` callers.

**Finding: No regression. Clean.**

The omission of `source_authority: community` is a forward-only change (new briefs omit it; existing briefs that already have it are not affected). Trailing `---` removal is safe because no Python script in the repo uses `yaml.safe_load_all` on skill-brief.yaml.

## Question 4: halt_reason Contract Consistency Across Scripts and SKILL.md

After all 5 PRs, SKILL.md's halt_reason enum (from the Result Contract section) is:
`null`, `input-missing`, `input-invalid`, `forge-tier-missing`, `target-inaccessible`, `gh-auth-failed`, `write-failed`, `overwrite-cancelled`

- `skf-validate-brief-inputs.py` (PR #275): emits `input-missing`, `input-invalid`, `null` — all present in enum. Clean.
- `skf-emit-brief-result-envelope.py` (PR #276): `VALID_HALT_REASONS` matches the SKILL.md enum exactly — all 8 values accounted for. Clean.
- `skf-write-skill-brief.py` (PR #276): exits with codes 0/1/2 (its own internal codes, not workflow-level halt_reasons) — it does not emit a halt_reason itself; `skf-emit-brief-result-envelope.py` is the authority on the envelope. Clean.

**Finding (MEDIUM): Exit code mismatch between `skf-write-skill-brief.py` and SKILL.md.**

`skf-write-skill-brief.py` exits 1 on validation failure and 2 on I/O failure. But step-05 maps these to SKILL.md's workflow-level exit codes 2 (`input-invalid`) and 4 (`write-failed`). The translation layer is implicit — step-05's markdown instructions are expected to map the script's exit 1 → `input-invalid` halt and exit 2 → `write-failed` halt. This mapping is not enforced in code; it lives only in prose. If a future step-05 revision misreads the mapping, it will emit the wrong workflow exit code. Confidence: 80. The correct fix is to document the translation table explicitly in step-05 or have `skf-write-skill-brief.py` output a `halt_reason` field in its own JSON response that `skf-emit-brief-result-envelope.py` can consume directly.

## Question 5: Frontmatter Paths vs. Lazy-Load Rule (PR #274)

The lazy-load rule added by PR #274 in SKILL.md states: "`references/*.md` and `assets/*.md` files are loaded inside the section that needs them, not at step entry."

- PR #275 adds `validateBriefInputsScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-validate-brief-inputs.py'` to step-01 frontmatter — this is a path interpolation variable, not a file read. The rule governs `references/*.md` and `assets/*.md` content loads. A frontmatter string constant is not a file load.
- PR #276 adds four frontmatter paths in step-05 (`writeSkillBriefScript`, `emitBriefEnvelopeScript`, `forgeTierRwScript`, `forgeTierFile`) — same analysis. These are interpolation defaults for bash invocations, not eager loads of `.md` reference files.

**Finding: No violation. Clean.** The lazy-load rule is scoped to `references/*.md` and `assets/*.md` markdown content loads. Script path strings in frontmatter are not file reads and are explicitly outside the rule's scope.

---

## Cross-PR review findings (merge-time)

The end-to-end review flagged **two HIGH merge-coordination items** and **one MEDIUM hardening opportunity**. None are code bugs — they only matter when you actually merge:

1. **HIGH — Merge order**: PR #275 and #276 both add `input-invalid` to SKILL.md with slightly different wording. PR #276's row is the superset (covers step-05 schema validation too). When merging, **pick #276's wording** at the conflict — or merge #276 first and #275 will rebase clean.

2. **HIGH — Step-01 collision**: PR #272 restructures step-01 §8 GATE into a table; PR #275 wires that GATE to the new validator script. **Recommended order: #272 first, then #275 needs a rebase** to apply its GATE changes against the table form. PRs #273 and #274 touch disjoint sections of step-01 and will rebase cleanly.

3. **MEDIUM — Implicit exit-code translation**: `skf-write-skill-brief.py` uses internal exit codes 0/1/2 (success/validation/IO), and step-05 §3 prose maps those to workflow halt_reasons (`input-invalid`/`write-failed`). The mapping lives only in prose — a future step-05 edit could mis-translate. Cleanest hardening: have `skf-write-skill-brief.py` emit `halt_reason` directly in its error JSON so the envelope script can consume it without translation. Worth a follow-up issue, not blocking.

## What I didn't do

- **Theme 4 finding 4** (extending `skf-extract-public-api.py` with `--repo+--ref` mode to eliminate the parent-reads-then-pipes pattern) — deferred as a structural change to a shared script. Worth a separate issue.
- **Recommendation #8** from the report (`--quick`, `--dry-run`, `description` direct-override, `[Q] Quit`) — explicitly out of scope; these are scope expansions, not findings.
